316 — Distributed SQL Query Optimizer (CockroachDB-style)

Project Number: 316

Project Name: Distributed SQL Query Optimizer (CockroachDB-style)

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a cost-based distributed SQL query optimizer (codename Quasar) in the CockroachDB / Cascades lineage: a memo of equivalent expressions, an optgen-generated rule engine (normalization + exploration + implementation), statistics-driven cardinality estimation (HyperLogLog NDV + equi-depth histograms), an index constraint/span solver, a distribution-aware cost model, a top-down branch-and-bound Cascades search producing the lowest-cost physical plan for required ordering + distribution, and a DistSQL flow planner that places processors/streams/routers onto nodes by range-leaseholder locality. It optimizes SQL into distributed physical plans; it does not parse full SQL, execute plans, store data, or run consensus — those are sibling projects.

1. Goal

1.1 Engineering Definition

Quasar = cost-based, Cascades-framework distributed SQL query optimizer for a distributed relational DB. Input: bound RelExpr (logical algebra from quasar-bind over a parsed statement). Pipeline:

  1. Insert expr into a Memo of equivalent plan-space exprs (groups of interchangeable logical/physical operators).
  2. Apply always-on normalization rules (confluent, cost-reducing canonicalizations) at construct time via factory hook.
  3. Run exploration (logical→logical) + implementation (logical→physical) rules driven by Cascades top-down branch-and-bound search over (GroupId × PhysProps) goal space.
  4. Estimate cardinality from HLL NDV + equi-depth histograms + multi-column stats; compute per-predicate selectivity; propagate through the tree.
  5. Cost physical alternatives via distribution-aware cost model (cpu+io+net) through the Coster trait.
  6. Pick lowest-cost physical MExpr satisfying root's required PhysProps (ordering+distribution); memoize winners per (GroupId, PhysProps).
  7. Lower winning plan into a DistSQL FlowSpec (processors, streams, routers) placed by range-leaseholder locality.
  8. Expose via optimize() -> PhysicalPlan + quasar CLI (explain, memo, opt-test, bench).
  • Deployment: embedded optimizer library (Rust crate) into a SQL layer; also standalone CLI.
  • State: each optimize() is self-contained, stateless — Memo, task stack, winner tables are ephemeral per-query heap allocs. quasar-cat + quasar-cache read-shared across concurrent calls; no shared mutable optimizer state.
  • Users: DB engineers integrating into a distributed SQL layer; perf engineers diagnosing plans via EXPLAIN (OPT)/(DISTSQL); operators reading FlowSpec for cross-node data movement.

1.2 System Boundaries

In scope:

Domain Detail
Relational + scalar IR RelExpr / ScalarExpr enums; ColumnId / GroupId / ExprId identifiers
Memo Group/MExpr arenas; interning + dedup; group logical props; winner tables; normalize-on-construct
optgen rule DSL .opt parser + Rust codegen via build.rs; normalization / exploration / implementation rules
Statistics Row counts; HLL NDV; equi-depth histograms; multi-column stats; null counts; selectivity; cardinality propagation
Index constraint/span solver Predicates over index key cols → Spans + remaining filter; tight/loose; IN/OR; inverted/partial indexes
Distribution-aware cost Per-operator cpu/io/seek/net; Coster trait; net dominates distributed plans
Cascades search + B&B Task stack (Optimize/Explore/ApplyRule/EnforceProps); branch-and-bound; winner memoization
PhysProps + enforcers Ordering (FD-simplified); Distribution; Sort + Distribute enforcers
Multi-region locality + LOS Leaseholder/range placement; locality-optimized search
DistSQL flow planning Physical plan → FlowSpec; ProcessorSpec; Stream; Router placement onto nodes
Plan cache + prepared stmts Generic vs custom plans; placeholder fast-path; memo staleness invalidation
EXPLAIN family (OPT) plan tree + costs; (DISTSQL) FlowSpec diagram; memo dump
Observability + replay Tracing spans; metrics; optimizer event log; deterministic replay harness
Conformance + fuzzing Datadriven golden files; TPC-H/TPC-DS-lite plan quality gating; rule/memo/cost fuzzing

Explicit non-goals:

Non-goal Disposition
Full SQL grammar quasar-sql is an intentional subset; full parser = projects 259/313
Execution engine Quasar emits FlowSpec; project 318 (Adaptive Query Execution) executes
Storage engine KV/ranges from 317 (Hybrid Row/Column) + 319 (Memory-Mapped); Quasar reads catalog metadata only
Transaction/consensus projects 196 (Distributed Txn Coordinator) + Raft; Quasar not transaction-aware
SQL wire protocol server no pg-wire, no connection management
DDL execution schema mutations are catalog inputs, not optimizer outputs
GPU/SIMD vectorized cost deferred PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25")
Online stats auto-collection daemon deferred PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25")
Cross-region 2PC cost modelling deferred PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §23")
Learned cardinality (ML) deferred PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25")

1.3 Phase 0 Deliverable — 16 Crate Stubs

Walking skeleton (Phase 0, §12) = cargo workspace with exactly these 16 crates under crates/:

# Crate Responsibility
1 quasar-cli quasar binary: optimize, EXPLAIN (plan/opt/distsql), memo dump, opt-test runner, bench
2 quasar-sql Small SQL lexer + parser → raw AST (front door; intentional subset)
3 quasar-cat Catalog: schemas, tables, columns, types, indexes (partial/inverted/computed), zone configs, regions/localities, range/leaseholder placement metadata
4 quasar-ir Relational algebra (RelExpr) + scalar algebra (ScalarExpr), Operator enum, ColumnId/ColSet
5 quasar-bind Optbuilder/binder: raw AST → bound RelExpr/ScalarExpr; name resolution, type checking, column-id allocation, subquery + correlation handling, FK/constraint hookup
6 quasar-props LogicalProps (out cols, not-null, keys, FDs, cardinality, outer cols) + PhysProps (ordering, distribution) + provided/required reconciliation
7 quasar-stats Statistics: row counts, NDV via HLL, equi-depth histograms, null counts, multi-column stats, avg row size; selectivity + cardinality estimation
8 quasar-memo The Memo: Group arena, MExpr arena, interning/dedup, group logical props, winners, normalize-on-construct factory hook
9 quasar-optgen Rule DSL compiler: parse .opt → generate Rust match/replace code at build time
10 quasar-rules Rule definitions (.opt sources) + generated dispatch: Normalize / Explore / Implement
11 quasar-constraint Constraint/span solver: predicates over index key cols → Spans + remaining filter; tight/loose
12 quasar-cost Cost model: per-operator CPU/IO/seek/net; Coster trait; distribution-aware cost
13 quasar-search Cascades search: task stack (Optimize/Explore/ApplyRule/EnforceProps), branch-and-bound, optimization goals, winner memoization, enforcer insertion, plan extraction
14 quasar-distsql Physical plan → distributed flow: Processor/Stream/Router specs, node placement by leaseholder locality, local-vs-distributed planning, ordered-stream merges
15 quasar-cache Plan/memo cache: prepared statements, placeholder fast-path, generic-vs-custom plan, memo fingerprint + schema/stats staleness invalidation
16 quasar-obs Observability: tracing spans, metrics, optimizer event log, deterministic replay harness

Phase 0 demo: quasar opt "SELECT a FROM t WHERE a > 1" runs E2E (parse → bind → 1-member memo → trivial cost → single TableReader FlowSpec → EXPLAIN). All other crates stub unimplemented!().

1.6 Timeline

1.7 Language Rationale — Rust (stable, 2021 edition)

Consideration Rust rationale
Memo = interned DAG, heavy structural sharing Arena + u32 handles (GroupId/ExprId) → pointer-free, GC-free sharing, deterministic latency
Algebraic enums RelExpr/ScalarExpr enum + exhaustive match; compiler catches unhandled operators when a rule is added
Borrow checker enforces memo discipline "append-mostly, props derived once, winners memoized" enforced at compile time
Predictable optimize() tail latency No GC pauses; arena allocation; deterministic
Rejected: Go CockroachDB's real choice; GC pauses + weaker sum types — interop lesson, not adopted
Rejected: C++ Memory-unsafe for an intricate shared DAG
Rejected: OCaml/Haskell Strong ADTs, weaker ops/ecosystem for this portfolio

1.8 Required Prerequisites

Domain Knowledge needed
Relational algebra Selection, projection, join, aggregation, set ops; operator trees
Query optimization theory Selinger DP, Volcano, Cascades; memo structure; rule systems
Statistics + estimation Histograms, NDV, HyperLogLog, selectivity, q-error
Distributed systems Partitioning, leaseholders, region placement, data locality, fan-out cost
Compilers / IR DAG IR, interning, normalization, codegen from DSL
Rust ownership + lifetimes Arena allocation, newtype patterns, trait objects, build.rs proc-macro patterns
Concurrency Read-shared catalog/cache; per-query isolated memo; lock discipline

2. Why Build This

2.1 Unique Systems Knowledge Taught

Requires implementing, debugging, and validating each component against real query workloads — not acquirable from textbooks alone.

Topic What building it teaches
Cascades framework Top-down memoized search with task stacks; why Cascades beats bottom-up DP for extensibility; group/expr/winner interaction; enforcer insertion mechanics
Memoization of plan space Structural sharing across exponential alternatives; arena allocation for DAGs; interning correctness; why memo size is bounded by join order × rule count
Cost-based search + B&B Pruning before full enumeration; cost budgets; winner propagation; why an order-of-magnitude cost error can still select the right plan
Cardinality estimation "Hardest unsolved problem in databases" (Leis et al. 2015); selectivity independence assumptions + failure modes; histogram bucket interpolation; multi-column correlation; why bad estimates cascade into catastrophic plans
Distribution-aware planning Net cost dominates cpu/io at cross-region scale; Distribution as first-class PhysProp; LOS; lookup join vs hash join distributed
PhysProps + enforcers Separating required vs provided; how Sort/Distribute enforcers fill gaps; interesting orderings from GROUP BY/ORDER BY/merge-join
Rule DSL codegen Mini-compiler (optgen): .opt → build-time Rust match/replace dispatch; pattern variables, guards, binding; why generated dispatch beats hand-written on maintainability
Functional dependency reasoning FD closure; equivalency classes; constant columns; keys; how FDs simplify ordering choices + enable projection pruning

2.2 Historical and Architectural Significance

Selinger et al. 1979 (System R)
  → bottom-up DP over join subsets, access path selection, "interesting orders"
  → Foundational: still the mental model for most DB courses

Graefe & McKenna 1993 (Volcano Optimizer Generator)
  → top-down recursive search, logical/physical rules, cost functions, physical properties
  → Introduced rule-based extensible optimizer architecture

Graefe 1995 (Cascades Framework)
  → memoization over Volcano, tasks + task stack, branch-and-bound, optimization goals
  → Direct ancestor of SQL Server's optimizer; Quasar's quasar-search is Cascades

Calcite (Apache, 2014)
  → JVM-based, RBO + CBO, planner rules, RelTrait system for physical properties
  → Deployed in Hive, Flink, Drill; Cascades-style in Java ecosystem

GPORCA / Orca (Soliman et al. 2014, SIGMOD)
  → Standalone optimizer for Greenplum/HAWQ; separated from executor; distribution-aware
  → Closest architectural peer: Quasar inherits "optimizer-as-library" deployment

CockroachDB optimizer (Taft et al. 2020, SIGMOD)
  → Cascades/Volcano-style in Go; optgen DSL; memo; normalization vs xform separation;
     DistSQL flow planning; locality-optimized search
  → PRIMARY REFERENCE; Quasar = clean-room Rust re-expression

Quasar (this project)
  → Rust, GC-free arena memo, compiler-enforced invariants, distribution as first-class property
  → Portfolio-complete: full Cascades stack from IR to DistSQL FlowSpec

2.3 Engineering Muscles Developed

Muscle How Quasar develops it
Recursive search with memoization Cascades task stack; OptimizeGroup recursion with memoized winner lookup
Branch-and-bound pruning Cost budget enforcement in quasar-search; early task termination
Property derivation LogicalProps + PhysProps derivation per operator in quasar-props
Statistics integration HLL sketch ingestion → NDV → selectivity → cardinality in quasar-stats
Code generation via DSL optgen: .opt parser → build.rs → generated Rust dispatch in quasar-optgen
Arena-based DAG allocation Group/MExpr/ScalarExpr arenas in quasar-memo with u32 handles
Datadriven testing discipline Golden file conformance suite (opt-test directives) in quasar-cli
Distributed cost reasoning Net-cost dominance; leaseholder placement; LOS in quasar-cost + quasar-distsql

2.4 Production Concepts Mastered

Concept Where it appears in Quasar
Plan stability Deterministic seed; stable rule ordering; plan cache generic-vs-custom logic (quasar-cache)
Prepared-statement plan cache Placeholder binding; memo fingerprint; schema + stats staleness invalidation (§25)
EXPLAIN as operator interface (OPT): plan tree + per-node cost; (DISTSQL): FlowSpec diagram; both in quasar-cli
Plan-quality regression gating TPC-H q1–q22-lite + TPC-DS-lite golden plan files; automated CI diff (§25)
Observability by default quasar-obs wraps every optimize(); tracing spans per task; optimizer event log; deterministic replay
Budget enforcement Memo memory ceiling + B&B cost budget → OptError::BudgetExceeded; never silently degrades

2.5 Portfolio Cross-Links

Project # Relationship to Quasar
Query Optimizer + Cost Estimator 313 Single-node sibling; Quasar adds distribution, DistSQL, LOS on the same Cascades skeleton
Distributed Transaction Coordinator 196 TrueTime + commit-protocol context; 2PC cost deferred §23
Hybrid Row/Column Storage 317 KV/range primitives Quasar reads as catalog metadata (leaseholders, range spans)
Adaptive Query Execution 318 Downstream executor consuming Quasar's FlowSpec; re-optimization hooks land here
Memory-Mapped DB 319 Lower-level storage; informs scan/seek cost constants in quasar-cost
SQL Database (full stack) — Quasar is the optimizer layer; SQL-Database integrates all layers E2E
JavaScript Parser + Runtime 259 Shared muscle: parser → IR → optimization passes mirrors SQL → RelExpr → rules
SSA-Optimizer / AST-Optimizer — Shared muscle: rewrite-rule systems, IR normalization, cost-driven pass ordering

2.6 Calibration Value

A developer who completes Quasar can:

  • Read + contribute to CockroachDB pkg/sql/opt, Apache Calcite planner, or GPORCA source.
  • Design + cost distributed query plans from first principles (join order, distribution strategy, enforcer placement).
  • Diagnose cardinality estimation failures from EXPLAIN; improve histograms/multi-column stats.
  • Write new optimizer rules in an optgen DSL; reason about confluence + correctness.
  • Build observability + deterministic replay into any complex search system.
  • Reason about plan stability, cache invalidation, generic-vs-custom plan tradeoff in prepared statements.

Permanently load-bearing for DB internals, query processing, distributed system design — not deprecated by framework or language churn.

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

After completing every phase, the practitioner holds these exact competencies:

  • Memo + structural interning (Phase 3 / §15): Can design and implement a pointer-free DAG memo using typed arenas and u32 handles; explain why arena allocation with structural dedup prevents redundant exploration and guarantees deterministic B&B pruning.
  • Cascades search + B&B (Phase 10 / §22): Can drive a top-down task-stack optimizer from scratch; derive required physical properties for every operator, insert enforcers correctly, and prove that the branch-and-bound is sound under cost monotonicity (I4/I7).
  • Cardinality estimation (Phase 5 / §17): Can implement equi-depth histograms and HyperLogLog NDV estimation; reason about q-error, independence assumptions, and when multi-column statistics are load-bearing for plan quality (Leis et al. 2015).
  • Rule DSL codegen (Phase 4 / §16): Can author a build-time Rust code-generator over a pattern/replacement DSL; enforce normalization idempotency and confluence at test time; audit why generated dispatch beats a runtime interpreter for a 200-rule set.
  • Distribution as first-class property (Phase 11 / §23): Can express hash and range partitioning as PhysProps, cost cross-region network transfer, and reason about when a LookupJoin beats a HashJoin in a geo-distributed setting.
  • Constraint/span solving (Phase 6 / §18): Can reduce arbitrary predicate conjunctions over composite indexes to sorted disjoint key spans; classify tightness; handle IN-lists, OR-unions, inverted indexes, and partial-index applicability.
  • Deterministic replay and observability (Phase 13 / §25): Can instrument a complex multi-phase computation with structured tracing, capture a deterministic event log, and build a replay harness that proves byte-identical re-execution — the foundation for any plan-regression postmortem workflow.
  • DistSQL flow planning (Phase 12 / §24): Can lower a winning physical plan into a FlowSpec by assigning processors to nodes using leaseholder locality, selecting routers (ByHash/ByRange/Mirror/PassThrough), and ensuring ordered fan-in merges maintain the required ordering property end-to-end.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Architecture Diagram

   SQL text
     |
     v
 +--------------+  raw AST   +--------------+  bound RelExpr/ScalarExpr (logical)
 | quasar-sql   |----------> | quasar-bind  |------------------------------+
 | lexer+parser |            | optbuilder   |<-- catalog -- quasar-cat     |
 +--------------+            +--------------+   (tables/indexes/zones/      |
                                                 regions/leaseholders)     |
                                                                           v
 +--------------------------- quasar-memo (Groups x MExprs, interned DAG) ----------------------------+
 |  normalize-on-construct <-- quasar-rules[Normalize] <-- quasar-optgen (.opt -> generated Rust)     |
 |  logical props <-- quasar-props (FDs, keys, not-null, cardinality, outer cols)                     |
 |  group statistics <-- quasar-stats (HLL NDV, equi-depth histograms, multi-col, selectivity)        |
 +---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
     |                                                ^
     | required physical props (ordering, distribution)| Explore[logical] + Implement[logical->physical]
     v                                                | rules (quasar-rules) . index spans (quasar-constraint)
 +--------------+  tasks: OptimizeGroup/Expr,         | . cost (quasar-cost: cpu/io/net/distribution)
 | quasar-search|  ExploreGroup/Expr, ApplyRule,      |
 |  Cascades    |  EnforceProps; branch-and-bound ----+
 |  B&B engine  |
 +--------------+
     | lowest-cost physical MExpr for the root's required props
     v
 +--------------+  processors + streams + routers   +--------------+
 |quasar-distsql| placed by leaseholder locality    |quasar-explain| EXPLAIN / (OPT) / (DISTSQL)
 | flow planner |---------------------------------> |  (in cli)    |
 +--------------+                                    +--------------+
     |                                                       ^
     v distributed PhysicalPlan (FlowSpec)                   | memo reuse + invalidation
 +--------------+                                    +--------------+
 | downstream:  |                                    | quasar-cache | prepared stmts, placeholders
 | exec (318)   |                                    +--------------+
 +--------------+
   quasar-obs (tracing . metrics . optimizer event log . deterministic replay) wraps the whole pipeline

3.2 Per-Component Dense Description

quasar-sql — Lexer + recursive-descent parser for an intentional SQL subset. In: UTF-8 SQL text. Out: RawStmt AST (untyped; no name resolution, no type inference). Inv: parser never touches catalog/type system; all errors purely syntactic. Extended grammar = projects 259/313.

quasar-bind — Optbuilder/binder: RawStmt → bound RelExpr/ScalarExpr. Does name resolution (table aliases, column refs), type checking, ColumnId allocation (globally unique u32 per output col), subquery unnesting markers, correlation detection, FK/constraint hookup. In: RawStmt + quasar-cat handle. Out: root RelExpr, all ColumnIds assigned. Inv: every output ColumnId is catalog-sourced or freshly allocated; no dangling refs.

quasar-cat — Catalog: schemas, tables, columns, types, indexes (partial/inverted/computed), zone configs, regions/localities, range/leaseholder placement. Read-only to optimizer (DDL out of scope). In: schema store (in-memory tests; pluggable trait for integration). Out: TableDescriptor, IndexDescriptor, ZoneConfig, RegionSet, RangeMeta for quasar-bind/-constraint/-distsql. Inv: catalog snapshot fixed for one optimize() lifetime; concurrent mutations do not affect in-flight optimization.

quasar-ir — Defines RelExpr/ScalarExpr enums + operator structs (ScanOp, IndexScanOp, JoinOp, LookupJoinOp, MergeJoinOp, ...); ColumnId, ColSet (compact bitset), TableId, IndexId, Datum. No execution logic. Used by quasar-bind (construct) + quasar-rules (match patterns). Inv: enums avoid #[non_exhaustive] — adding a variant forces all match arms updated at compile time, catching missing rule coverage.

quasar-memo — The Memo: Group arena + MExpr arena + scalar arena, interning HashMap (MExprKey → ExprId), per-group winner table (PhysProps → Winner). Factory memo.add_expr(op, children, scalar) runs normalization immediately + dedups structurally identical exprs before insert. In: RelExpr/Operator + child GroupIds. Out: (GroupId, ExprId). Inv: logically identical exprs → same ExprId (structural dedup); LogicalProps immutable once computed; winners set-only, never cleared.

quasar-props — Derives + stores LogicalProps (out_cols, not_null, keys, FD closure, cardinality bounds, outer_cols) + PhysProps (ordering choice, distribution). LogicalProps computed once per group on first member insert + cached. PhysProps = required-vs-provided reconciliation domain for quasar-search. FD closure includes equivalency groups, constant columns, strict FDs. Inv: LogicalProps derivation = pure function of operator + child group props; no side effects.

quasar-stats — Statistics: row counts, HLL-derived NDV (distinct_count), equi-depth histograms (Bucket: upper_bound, num_eq, num_range, distinct_range), null counts, multi-column stats, avg row size. Selectivity estimators: range (histogram interpolation), equality (NDV), IN-list, IS NULL, LIKE prefix, join (min-of-NDV heuristic + multi-column). Cardinality propagated through project/select/join/group-by. Inv: missing stats → is_estimated = true; never crash; degrade to bounded heuristics (row_count = 1000, selectivity = 0.1).

quasar-optgen — Build-time rule DSL compiler. At build.rs time reads .opt files in quasar-rules, parses rule syntax (name, kind tag, pattern tree, replacement tree), emits Rust match arms + replacement construction into generated/rules.rs. In: .opt sources. Out: generated Rust via include!. Inv: generated code = pure function of .opt sources; regenerated on any change; never hand-edited.

quasar-rules — .opt source rule defs + generated dispatch. Normalization: constant folding, predicate merge/pushdown, projection pruning, simplification, fold/eliminate. Exploration: join commutativity/associativity, join-order xforms, GenerateIndexScans, predicate/limit pushdown variants, GroupBy/Distinct rewrites. Implementation: Scan→TableReader, Join→HashJoin/MergeJoin/LookupJoin, GroupBy→HashAgg/StreamAgg, Sort→Sorter enforcer, Distribute enforcer. Dispatch entrypoint consumed by quasar-memo (Normalize) + quasar-search (Explore/Implement). Inv: every normalization rule is confluent + cost-reducing (never enlarges or ties expr size); enforced by conformance suite.

quasar-constraint — Constraint/span solver. Given a predicate conjunction + an index's key column list, derives Spans (sorted Span { start, start_boundary, end, end_boundary }) covering rows satisfying prefix-column predicates, plus a remaining scalar filter for non-prefix predicates. Tight = spans exactly match predicate (no false positives → no post-filter). Handles: range, equality, IN-list (→ point spans), OR (span union), IS NULL, computed columns, partial index predicates, inverted index applicability. Inv: tight constraint → remaining is TRUE; loose → remaining must be applied.

quasar-cost — Per-operator cost model via Coster: fn cost(&self, memo: &Memo, expr: ExprId, required: &PhysProps) -> Cost. Components: cpu (tuple processing), io (page reads/seeks), net (cross-node bytes); total is ordering key; net dominates distributed. Distribution-aware: cross-region net applies a latency multiplier. In: operator type, cardinality from group LogicalProps.stats, PhysProps (distribution), index descriptor (seek cost). Inv: cost = deterministic pure function of memo state + required props; no randomness; no syscalls.

quasar-search — Cascades search engine. Vec<Task> stack; types: OptimizeGroup, OptimizeExpr, ExploreGroup, ExploreExpr, ApplyRule, EnforceProps. B&B: tracks cost budget per goal; tasks for goals whose current-best already exceeds budget are pruned. Winners in Group.winners[PhysProps]. Plan extraction: walk winner chain from root. EnforceProps inserts Sort/Distribute enforcers when required props unprovided by any member. Inv: no Task pushed for a (group, req) pair whose winner cost is already below the current best sibling; B&B pruning is monotone.

quasar-distsql — Lowers winning physical plan (quasar-search winner chain) into a FlowSpec: Vec<ProcessorSpec> + Vec<Stream>. Node placement: each ProcessorSpec → NodeId via leaseholder locality from quasar-cat (scans → leaseholder node for the key range; joins → build-side node; aggregators follow data). Routers: ByHash (hash-distributed joins), ByRange (range-distributed scans), PassThrough (local pipelines), Mirror (broadcast). Local-vs-distributed: all ranges on one node → local (gateway-only) flow. Inv: every Stream connects a valid (source ProcId, dest ProcId); no dangling streams.

quasar-explain (in quasar-cli) — Renders optimizer output as text or JSON. (OPT): annotated plan tree — operator, output columns, cardinality estimate, per-node cost. (DISTSQL): FlowSpec diagram — processors, streams, routers, node placement. quasar memo: group table with members, logical props, winners per PhysProps. Not a separate crate; rendering logic in quasar-cli. Inv: EXPLAIN output deterministic given identical (memo, winners, FlowSpec); used as conformance golden output.

quasar-cache — Plan cache for prepared statements. (fingerprint → CachedPlan); fingerprint = normalized SQL text + catalog version + stats version. Generic plan: optimized with placeholder types, not values (placeholder selectivity uncertain). Custom plan: optimized with bound values (value selectivity matters). Generic-vs-custom chosen by plan cost variance across recent executions. Staleness: catalog schema change or stats refresh bumps version; stale entries evicted lazily. Inv: a cached plan is never served after its catalog or stats version is stale.

quasar-obs — Observability wrapper over the full pipeline. tracing spans per task (OptimizeGroup duration, rule applications, cost evaluations). Metrics: memo group count, MExpr count, rules fired, search tasks executed, optimization duration, plan cache hit/miss. Optimizer event log: structured record of every task + outcome + cost, serializable for replay. Replay harness: given an event log, replays optimization with same rule order + costs + stats → must produce identical plan. Inv: any original-vs-replay divergence is a bug; replay is the primary non-determinism debugging tool.

3.3 Boundary Enumeration

Trust boundary:

  • Untrusted: SQL text (arbitrary input; quasar-sql must not panic on malformed input), placeholder values (type-checked, user-supplied values), catalog statistics (from stats daemon; may be stale/absent).
  • Trusted core: memo, rules, search, cost — pure functions over validated inputs; no network I/O, no filesystem, no unsafe untrusted access.

Concurrency boundary:

  • optimize() is single-threaded per query — no shared mutable state in memo/search/rule engine.
  • quasar-cat + quasar-cache read-shared across concurrent calls — Arc<dyn CatalogReader> + Arc<Mutex<PlanCache>>.
  • No intra-optimization parallelism; parallel planning is caller-managed (one thread per query).

Persistence boundary:

  • Catalog + stats: read-only external inputs; Quasar never writes them.
  • Plan cache: in-memory only (quasar-cache); no disk persistence in scope.
  • Memo: per-query ephemeral heap; dropped after optimize() returns.
  • Event log: quasar-obs in-memory ring buffer; flushed to disk only on replay request.

Protocol boundary:

  • In: bound RelExpr tree (quasar-ir types) from quasar-bind.
  • Out: FlowSpec (quasar-distsql types) — serializable physical plan for execution engine (project 318).
  • EXPLAIN: UTF-8 text or JSON; for operators + tooling.
  • Errors: Result<PhysicalPlan, OptError> — all failures encoded in OptError; no panics on recoverable errors.

4. System Constraints

4.1 Latency Targets (optimize() wall-clock)

Query complexity p50 p95 p99 Notes
Simple 1–2 table (no join reorder) < 0.5 ms < 1 ms < 3 ms Index scan + select; memo < 20 groups
3–5 table join < 2 ms < 5 ms < 15 ms Exploration rules fire; ~50–200 groups
10-table join < 10 ms < 30 ms < 80 ms B&B kicks in; join order enumeration bounded
20-table join < 50 ms < 150 ms < 500 ms B&B budget enforced; may not find global optimum
Plan cache hit (generic reuse) < 0.05 ms < 0.1 ms < 0.3 ms Fingerprint lookup + placeholder bind only

Measured single-core, warm catalog, pre-loaded stats, no disk I/O during optimization.

4.2 Throughput Target

Metric Target
Simple (1–2 table) > 5,000 optimize() / sec / core
Medium (5-table) > 500 optimize() / sec / core
Plan cache hit > 50,000 lookups / sec / core

4.3 Memory Constraints

Resource Limit Enforcement
Memo per-query heap < 64 MB Memo.budget: Cost; arena byte counter → OptError::BudgetExceeded
Group count / query ≤ 32,768 GroupId u32; practical limit = memo budget
MExpr count / query ≤ 131,072 ExprId u32; practical limit = memo budget
ScalarExpr count / query ≤ 65,536 ScalarId u32
Plan cache size default 256 MB (configurable) LRU eviction; staleness invalidation
ColSet width fixed bitset over ColumnId u32; ~1024 active cols/query overflow → OptError::Unsupported

Hard cap exceeded → OptError::BudgetExceeded(cost) with partial-result annotation in event log; never OOM.

4.4 Query Complexity Limits

Dimension Limit Rationale
Reorderable join count ≤ 32 Join enumeration exponential; B&B + DPhyp limit; beyond → heuristic ordering
Column count / query ≤ ~1024 ColSet fixed-width compact bitset
Index key column count ≤ 16 / index Constraint solver span enumeration; IN-list fan-out
IN-list size before span collapse ≤ 10,000 values Beyond → single full-range span + remaining filter
Subquery nesting depth ≤ 20 Recursive bind stack depth; configurable
Window function partitions ≤ 32 partition keys Sorter cost model assumption
Max regions / distribution ≤ 9 Distribution::Set = SmallVec<[RegionId; 4]>; beyond → Distribution::Any
Max ranges / scan ≤ 1024 quasar-distsql span enumeration cap

Violations → OptError::Unsupported with descriptive message; fallback to heuristic plan where possible.

4.5 Startup and Initialization

Metric Target
quasar-cli cold start (catalog + stats from file) < 50 ms
Catalog schema load (100 tables, 10 indexes each) < 10 ms
Stats load (100 tables, all histograms) < 20 ms
optgen rule table init < 1 ms (static dispatch; no runtime parse)

4.6 Determinism Requirements

Input set Guarantee
Identical (sql_text, catalog_snapshot, stats_snapshot, seed) Byte-identical plan output; identical FlowSpec
Identical inputs, different concurrent-query execution order No effect (per-query isolated memo)
Rule application order Stable: rules in RuleId order within a group; RuleId assigned lexicographically from .opt file order
Cost tie-breaking Deterministic: ties broken by ExprId ascending (memo insertion order)
HLL sketches Seeded with Optimizer.seed; NDV deterministic given same seed + input

Determinism = primary debugging + replay guarantee. quasar-obs event log + replay harness must produce identical plan.

4.7 Cardinality Estimation Quality

Metric Target
Q-error on TPC-H (= max(est/true, true/est)) Median < 5×; 90th pct < 50×
TPC-H join plan quality vs PostgreSQL Within 2× cost on ≥ 18 of 22 queries
TPC-DS-lite plan stability No regression across stats refreshes within 10% row-count change
Missing stats fallback Never crash; is_estimated = true, heuristic row_count = 1000, selectivity = 0.1

4.8 Cost Model Stability

Property Requirement
Cost type f64 components (cpu, io, net, total)
Platform portability No f32 narrowing; no std::f64::NAN in comparisons; total always finite
FP determinism Same inputs → same f64 on same platform (no thread-local FP state)
Cost ordering total sole comparison key; ties broken by ExprId, not component values
No negative costs All components ≥ 0.0; asserted at construction

4.9 Branch-and-Bound Budget Knobs

Knob Default Range Effect
memo_byte_budget 64 MB 1 MB – 512 MB Hard stop on memo growth
task_budget 100,000 tasks 1,000 – 10,000,000 Hard stop on search tasks
cost_budget ∞ (no limit) > 0.0 Prune branches exceeding cost
join_reorder_limit 12 4 – 32 DPhyp cutoff; beyond → left-deep heuristic
exploration_depth 5 1 – 20 Max recursive exploration rule depth per group

All knobs are per-optimize() parameters; not global mutable state.

4.10 Availability and Scaling

Concern Disposition
Availability / RTO N/A — library, no daemon, no network service; caller manages availability
Horizontal scaling Stateless replicas: each query thread runs its own optimize() + memo; no shared state except read-only catalog + plan cache
Vertical scaling Larger memo_byte_budget → more complex queries; more cores → more concurrent optimize()
CPU / cache Memo arena cache-friendly (u32 GroupId/ExprId; sequential arena alloc); Group struct ~128 bytes; hot path in L2
NUMA Not addressed; single-socket assumed for core; caller does NUMA-aware thread pinning
