6. Design Principles

12 non-negotiable principles. Each: statement + tradeoff + operational implication + violation failure mode. Referenced elsewhere by number ("per Principle 3").

1. Cost-Based Over Heuristic

Every non-trivial plan choice decided by comparing Cost.total:

Decision Options compared by cost
Join algorithm HashJoin vs MergeJoin vs LookupJoin
Join order all permutations within B&B budget (DPhyp up to join_reorder_limit)
Access method Scan (TableReader) vs IndexScan (one+ indexes)
Aggregation HashAgg vs StreamAgg (sorted input)
Distribution local-only vs distributed; hash-partition vs broadcast

Heuristics only in declared fallbacks: reorder-limit exceeded → left-deep; missing stats → bounded estimate is_estimated=true. All fallbacks logged in quasar-obs.

  • Tradeoff: slower + more complex than rule-based; needs accurate stats.
  • Operational: cardinality regression silently degrades plans → gated by TPC-H/TPC-DS-lite golden suite every CI run.
  • Violation: hard "always index scan" rule bypasses cost model → breaks low-selectivity predicates, hot-row scans, small tables (seek cost > scan cost).

2. Memo Is the Single Source of Truth

All equivalent alternatives, logical props, physical props, winner decisions live exclusively in Memo (quasar-memo). No plan decision inspects un-memoized exprs outside quasar-search. No prop (cardinality/FD/ordering) recomputed in a rule or cost fn — always read group LogicalProps.

  • Props: computed once per group, at first member insertion, in quasar-props; immutable thereafter.

  • Winners: Group.winners: HashMap<PhysProps, Winner>; set once, never cleared.

  • Rules read group props from memo; never recompute.

  • Tradeoff: all reasoning serializes through memo → no parallel prop derivation within one optimize().

  • Operational: debug any plan decision via memo dump (quasar memo); dump is ground truth.

  • Violation: rule computing cardinality inline without reading memo.group(id).logical.stats → shadow truth diverging under stats updates → inconsistent costs for equivalent exprs.

3. Normalization and Exploration Strictly Separated

Rule kind Fires Confluent Cost constraint Triggered by
Normalize always, in memo.add_expr factory, pre-insertion yes — any order → same canonical form cost-reducing or canonicalizing; never size-increasing memo construction (quasar-bind, rule outputs)
Explore (logical→logical) in ExploreExpr task, cost-driven no may explore larger exprs, later pruned ExploreGroup/ExploreExpr
Implement (logical→physical) in OptimizeExpr task, cost-driven no physical alts may be costlier pre-prune OptimizeExpr

  • Normalize examples: Select(Select(in,f1),f2) → Select(in,And(f1,f2)); constant folding; trivial projection elimination.

  • Explore examples: join commutativity, join associativity, GenerateIndexScans.

  • Tradeoff: normalize cannot be context-sensitive (no cost budget, no props); explore can be selective.

  • Operational: non-confluent normalize → non-deterministic memo by insertion order → caught by norm directive in conformance suite.

  • Violation: normalize generating strictly larger expr (e.g. Select(in,f) → Select(Select(in,f),TRUE)) → loops with its inverse → exhausts memo budget.

4. Determinism Over Convenience

Identical (sql_text, catalog_snapshot, stats_snapshot, seed) →

  • byte-identical plan, FlowSpec, EXPLAIN text.
  • Rule order: RuleId ascending (lexicographic from .opt file order).
  • Tie-break: ExprId ascending (memo arena insertion order).
  • HLL sketches: seeded by Optimizer.seed (u64) → same seed = same NDV.
  • No thread-local state in any optimizer path.
  • No HashMap::iter() in any plan-affecting path — use BTreeMap/insertion-ordered IndexMap.

Determinism checklist (CI-enforced):

Non-determinism source Mitigation
HashMap iteration order BTreeMap in winner table, rule dispatch, equiv-class sets
FP rounding platform variation only f64; no mixed precision; no NAN in comparisons
Thread scheduling optimize() single-threaded; no concurrent tasks
Clock / random only Optimizer.seed; no rand::thread_rng()
Pointer identity GroupId/ExprId are u32 arena indices, not pointers
  • Tradeoff: BTreeMap O(log n) vs HashMap O(1) — negligible vs rule-application cost.
  • Operational: non-determinism detected by replay harness (quasar-obs); P0 bug, root-cause before other work.
  • Violation: HashMap::iter() order in rule priority queue → plans differ between runs → defeats replay, makes perf regressions unattributable.

5. Physical Properties Drive Plan Choice

Required props propagate top-down; provided props bottom-up. No physical operator selected without reconciliation.

required props ──► quasar-search ──► OptimizeExpr
                                        │ check: provided ⊇ required?
                              yes ──► cost + memoize winner
                              no  ──► EnforceProps task → enforcer (Sort/Distribute)

  • Ordering: OrderingChoice { cols: Vec<OrderingCol> }, FD-simplified (constant/equiv cols dropped). Interesting orderings: ORDER BY, GROUP BY key, merge-join key, index order.

  • Distribution: Distribution { Any | Single(RegionId) | Set(SmallVec<[RegionId;4]>) }; Distribute enforcer inserts hash/range-shuffle processor.

  • Provided: declared by each physical op's implementation rule; stored in Winner.provided.

  • Required: passed into OptimizeGroup; children get derived requirements.

  • Tradeoff: enforcer insertion adds cost (Sort O(n log n) CPU; Distribute net bandwidth) — correctly models real property-establishment cost.

  • Operational: wrong provided_distribution → incorrect results at exec with no optimizer error → validated by execution integration tests.

  • Violation: MergeJoin selected without verifying input ordering → semantically incorrect plan on unsorted inputs → executor silently wrong.

6. Every Search Is Bounded

Three independent termination bounds:

Bound Knob Default Enforcement On breach
Memo memory memo_byte_budget 64 MB arena byte counter at every add_expr OptError::BudgetExceeded
Task count task_budget 100,000 decrement per task pop; halt at 0 OptError::BudgetExceeded
B&B cost cost_budget (per goal) ∞ default current lower-bound vs incumbent winner; prune if ≥ skip task; log

On BudgetExceeded: return best plan so far (may be suboptimal); annotate EXPLAIN "plan may be suboptimal (budget exhausted)"; log in quasar-obs.

  • Tradeoff: aggressive budgets miss global optimum; loose budgets cause latency spikes.
  • Operational: BudgetExceeded rate is key metric; high rate → under-budgeted queries or pathological SQL needing redesign.
  • Violation: removing task_budget check for "complex queries" → adversarial SQL (30-table cross-join) OOMs/stalls optimizer → violates §4.1 latency guarantees.

7. Statistics Honesty

Optimizer never crashes on missing/stale stats. All degradation explicit + logged.

Missing data Fallback Flag
Table row count 1000.0 Statistics.is_estimated=true
Column NDV min(1000.0, row_count) ColumnStatistic no histogram
Histogram None (NDV-based selectivity) is_estimated=true
Multi-column stats independence assumption is_estimated=true
Join stats min(left,right) × min-NDV selectivity is_estimated=true

Cardinality monotonicity: Select Cardinality.max ≤ input Cardinality.max (filter cannot raise upper bound); enforced in quasar-props derivation.

  • Tradeoff: heuristic fallbacks → poor plans on large un-analyzed tables; accepted as "run ANALYZE" signal.
  • Operational: EXPLAIN shows (estimated) when is_estimated=true; alert when is_estimated ratio > threshold.
  • Violation: panic on None histogram in join cardinality → optimizer unavailable for any query on a new table pre-ANALYZE → severe availability regression.

8. Distribution Is a First-Class Physical Property

Not post-processed after plan selection — a member of PhysProps, considered during Cascades search.

PhysProps { ordering: OrderingChoice, distribution: Distribution, limit_hint: f64 }
  • Every physical implementation rule declares provided_distribution.
  • Every join/agg/sort rule has separate variants for Any, Single(r), Set(regions).
  • Distribute enforcer: inserts ProcessorCore::Noop with Router::ByHash/Router::ByRange to reshuffle.
  • Cost: net cost on cross-node movement; LOS prefers Single(gateway_region) when all data local.
Distribution decision Cost driver Preferred alternative
Hash join build side net cost to ship build relation to each probe node broadcast if build small
Cross-region sort net cost to consolidate at gateway ordered-stream merge at gateway
Lookup join net: correlated lookup per input row prefer when input cardinality low
  • Tradeoff: every implementation rule written distribution-aware → more rules.
  • Operational: new operator omitting provided_distribution → incorrect distribution metadata → data-correctness failure at exec.
  • Violation: computing net cost only in quasar-distsql (post-selection) → cannot choose distributed vs non-distributed → always picks distributed regardless of locality.

9. Generated Rules Over Hand-Written Dispatch

All normalize/explore/implement rules defined in .opt files under crates/quasar-rules/rules/. quasar-optgen compiles them at build time (build.rs) into crates/quasar-rules/src/generated/rules.rs.

.opt syntax (illustrative):

#[PushSelectIntoJoin, Explore]
(Select (InnerJoin $left:* $right:* $on:*) $filter:*)
    => (InnerJoin (Select $left $filter) $right $on)
    when (ColsSubset (FilterCols $filter) (OutputCols $left))

Audit trail: every rule application in quasar-obs records RuleDef.name (.opt source), not generated fn name.

  • Tradeoff: build-time codegen adds CI complexity; needs working optgen before rules function.
  • Operational: .opt files are canonical rule docs; reviewers read .opt, not generated/rules.rs (checked in for reproducibility, not hand-edited).
  • Violation: hand-written Rust match bypass → invisible to .opt tooling, excluded from event log, un-fuzzable by rule fuzzer.

10. Observability and Replayability by Default

Every optimize() instrumented by quasar-obs without caller opt-in.

Signal Implementation Cost Default
tracing spans one span per task type (OptimizeGroup, ApplyRule, …) ~50 ns/span always-on (sampled in prod)
Metrics memo_groups, memo_exprs, rules_fired, tasks_executed, optimize_duration_ms ~5 ns/incr always-on
Event log structured record/task: type, args, cost, winner set ~200 ns/event + serde off; OptimizerConfig.event_log=true
Deterministic replay re-execute from event log; assert plan identity = original optimize() on demand / on mismatch

Replay contract: event log → byte-identical FlowSpec. Any divergence = P0 non-determinism bug.

  • Tradeoff: event log serde ~200 ns/event × 100k tasks ≈ 20 ms on complex queries → off by default in prod fast path.
  • Operational: any surprising plan diagnosed by enabling event log on a replica + replaying → eliminates "cannot reproduce".
  • Violation: rule reading external clock/global counter → plan diverges on replay → replay harness useless.

11. Bounded Resource Usage Is Non-Negotiable

OptError::BudgetExceeded and OptError::Unsupported are first-class results, never panics. Optimizer never terminates via panic on well-formed input.

Resource Limit Check point Overflow
Memo bytes 64 MB (configurable) every add_expr BudgetExceeded
Group count u32::MAX (practical: memo byte limit) arena alloc BudgetExceeded
MExpr count u32::MAX (practical: memo byte limit) arena alloc BudgetExceeded
Task count 100,000 (configurable) task pop BudgetExceeded
Column count ColSet width (~1024) column alloc in quasar-bind Unsupported
Spans per constraint 1024 quasar-constraint span union collapse to single full-range span
Subquery nesting 20 quasar-bind recursive call Unsupported

All limits via OptimizerConfig — no global mutable state, no env vars, no compile-time constants needing recompilation.

  • Tradeoff: eager limit checks add per-allocation overhead — negligible vs alloc + derivation.
  • Operational: limits exposed in quasar-obs metrics (memo_byte_usage, task_count); alert >80% utilization.
  • Violation: unwrap() on GroupId alloc beyond range → optimizer crash indistinguishable from executor crash → unattributable without stack traces, hidden from EXPLAIN.

12. No Execution Coupling

Output is a FlowSpec. optimize() returns, then execution begins independently. No execution-engine callbacks during optimization.

optimize(bound_expr, catalog, stats, config) -> Result<FlowSpec, OptError>
  pure: reads catalog + stats; writes ephemeral memo; returns FlowSpec
  no network, no disk, no executor callbacks, no shared mutable state

Adaptive re-optimization (project 318) is a caller-level loop: executor detects runtime cardinality divergence → calls optimize() again with updated stats → fresh FlowSpec → resumes. Fresh call with new Memo; does not mutate prior FlowSpec in-place.

  • Tradeoff: no intra-optimization runtime feedback; cannot correct cardinality errors mid-execution within one call.
  • Operational: re-optimization needs caller to update stats snapshot + re-invoke; quasar-cache generic-vs-custom logic handles cost/stability tradeoff.
  • Violation: rule calling into execution engine to probe row counts → optimizer non-embeddable in offline plan analysis, CI conformance, replay harnesses (all lack a live executor).

7. System Invariants

Load-bearing contracts I1..I12. Each stated once with enforcing crate, detection, telemetry, recovery, debug pathway.

Invariant Table

ID Invariant Statement Enforcing Crate / Type Detection Mechanism
I1 Identical (op, children, scalar) → unique ExprId quasar-memo: Memo::intern debug_assert in intern; collision audit in quasar-obs
I2 All MExpr members of a Group share identical LogicalProps quasar-memo: Group::add_member assert_eq derived props vs group.logical at add
I3 Normalization is idempotent and confluent quasar-rules [Normalize] + quasar-memo factory normalize(normalize(e)) == normalize(e)
I4 Parent cost ≥ sum of child costs (monotonicity) quasar-cost: Coster::cost post-cost assertion; regression fixtures
I5 Winner satisfies required PhysProps or NoPlanFound quasar-search: OptimizeGroup checked at winner extraction; enforcer loop terminates
I6 OrderingChoice only references FD-closed column set quasar-props: OrderingChoice::simplify debug_assert in simplify; FD closure reachability
I7 B&B never returns a plan worse than exhaustive within budget quasar-search exhaustive reference run; cost comparison
I8 Generic plan valid for all legal placeholder bindings quasar-cache proptest over typed placeholder domains
I9 Memo append-only post-construction; no mutation post winner extraction quasar-memo Rust ownership: Memo consumed into Winner
I10 Same (sql, catalog, stats, seed) → byte-identical plan quasar-obs replay harness replay: run twice, diff plan bytes
I11 FD closure consistent: key cols → all out_cols quasar-props: FuncDepSet::closure closure completeness assert in derive_logical_props
I12 Root winner Distribution matches required Distribution quasar-search: EnforceProps enforcer insertion check; post-extraction assert

I1 — Memo Structural Dedup

Field Detail
Statement For any pair of MExpr with equal (op, children: &[GroupId], scalar: Option<ScalarId>), only one ExprId exists in memo.
Why Without dedup: redundant members inflate rule-application cardinality; B&B explores duplicates; plan extraction ambiguous.
Enforced Memo::intern() hashes MExprKey { op_tag, children_ids, scalar_id } into intern: HashMap<MExprKey, ExprId>; key present → return existing ExprId, no alloc.
Detection debug_assert! on every intern (dev); Memo::check_dedup() re-hashes all members verifying uniqueness, called at E2E end; counter memo.intern.hit vs memo.intern.new — runaway ratio signals bug.
Telemetry memo.intern.hit, memo.intern.new, memo.expr_count gauge.
Recovery Logic error, not transient. Panic in debug; log + no-op in release (continue with duplicate — sub-optimal not unsafe). Post-run integrity check flags anomaly.
Debug QUASAR_MEMO_AUDIT=1; Memo::dump() emits JSON of all groups + member keys; diff vs expected to locate divergent intern.

I2 — Group Logical Props Uniformity

Field Detail
Statement group.logical derived from first MExpr added; every subsequent member must derive identical LogicalProps (out_cols, not_null, keys, FDs, cardinality, outer_cols).
Why Cascades uses group-level logical props for all rule match/cost; mismatch → rules see inconsistent column sets → invalid plan extraction.
Enforced Group::add_member(&mut self, expr: ExprId, derived: LogicalProps) -> Result<(), OptError> derives props, asserts derived == self.logical. Exploration rules add via memo factory, never raw arena push.
Detection debug_assert_eq!(derived.out_cols, self.logical.out_cols) + key/FD equivalence; integration test adds inequivalent member, expects Err(OptError::Unsupported).
Telemetry memo.props_mismatch counter (always-zero in prod; non-zero alerts).
Recovery OptError::Unsupported with diagnostic naming mismatching MExpr + group; producing rule logged.
Debug quasar explain --opt emits per-group props alongside members; compare manually.

I3 — Normalization Idempotency and Confluence

Field Detail
Statement (a) Idempotency: normalizing an already-normalized expr → same expr (no rewrite). (b) Confluence: any rule-application order → same normal form.
Why No idempotency → factory loops infinitely. No confluence → plan quality depends on rule order → non-deterministic across refactors.
Enforced [Normalize] rules cost-reducing/canonicalizing (non-expanding); factory applies them in fixed topological order (constants before predicates, pushdown before merge); CI quasar opt-test norm directive golden-diff.
Detection Idempotency: normalize each golden expr twice, assert bit-identical. Confluence: rule pair (A,B), A∘B vs B∘A, assert equal. Loop guard: step counter capped MAX_NORM_STEPS = 256 → exceed emits OptError::Unsupported.
Telemetry rules.normalize.steps histogram (P99 « cap when healthy).
Recovery Idempotency failure → offending rule fired on normalized form has bug; disable rule, return pre-rule expr, log rules.norm.idempotency_violation.
Debug QUASAR_RULES_TRACE=1 emits every match/replace to stderr with before/after expr.

I4 — Cost Monotonicity

Field Detail
Statement Physical MExpr with children c_1..c_n chosen costs: cost(expr).total >= sum_i cost(c_i).total. Adding a node (sort/hash join/agg) never decreases total cost.
Why B&B pruning relies on monotonicity: partial plan exceeding best cannot improve with more children. Violation → B&B prunes valid plans → sub-optimal results.
Enforced Coster::cost() — each operator adds non-negative delta to child-cost sum; CPU/IO/net all f64 >= 0; default BaseCoster never subtracts.
Detection Debug assert result.total >= child_cost_sum - EPSILON; per-operator golden fixture cost(op+children) >= children_sum; counter cost.monotonicity_violation (prod) → on-call alert.
Telemetry cost.monotonicity_violation counter; per-operator cost histograms.
Recovery Clamp operator cost to max(delta, 0.0); log bad operator + input cardinality for offline analysis.
Debug quasar explain --opt shows per-node costs; trace to node where cost decreases.

I5 — Required Physical Properties Satisfied or NoPlanFound

Field Detail
Statement Winner for (GroupId, required: PhysProps) either (a) has provided ⊇ required or (b) no winner → OptError::NoPlanFound. No third state.
Why Downstream (quasar-distsql, exec) relies on plan satisfying ordering+distribution; silent ordering violation → incorrect results (e.g. LIMIT returns arbitrary rows).
Enforced EnforceProps task: no member provides required → insert Sort/Distribute enforcer + re-optimize; extract_plan() asserts winner.provided.satisfies(required) before return.
Detection Assertion in extract_plan (debug + release); conformance test: ORDER BY + LIMIT vs golden ordered output in conformance/testdata/opt/.
Telemetry search.enforcer_inserted (Sort, Distribute tagged separately); search.no_plan_found.
Recovery Enforcer cannot satisfy (e.g. required Single(r) but no range on region r) → NoPlanFound with diagnostic.
Debug quasar explain --opt shows physical plan with enforcer nodes; inspect Sort/Distribute insertion.

I6 — OrderingChoice FD-Consistency

Field Detail
Statement After OrderingChoice::simplify(fds: &FuncDepSet), ordering contains only cols (a) not FD-constant and (b) not redundant given leading key; every col in out_cols.
Why Ordering including a constant col is vacuous (always satisfied) → search requests unnecessary Sort enforcers → inflated cost.
Enforced OrderingChoice::simplify() — strips FD-constant cols, collapses equiv groups, truncates at key prefix.
Detection `debug_assert!(choice.cols.iter().all(
Telemetry None needed; logic error caught in CI.
Recovery simplify called defensively at enforcer-insertion time; second simplify is no-op on a clean choice.
Debug quasar memo "<sql>" dumps per-winner PhysProps incl. ordering; compare vs FD set in logical props.

I7 — Branch-and-Bound Soundness

Field Detail
Statement Plan returned by B&B has cost ≤ cost of any complete physical plan discoverable within the same rule/task budget.
Why B&B pruning valid only when cost bound is a guaranteed lower bound for unexplored subtrees; too-aggressive bound prunes feasible lower-cost plans.
Enforced B&B upper bound init from root group's first complete plan cost; prune only when partial_cost >= current_best, never stricter; lower bound = sum of computed child costs (per I4).
Detection Reference test ≤5 tables: exhaustive enumeration (pruning off) vs B&B, equal within float epsilon; TPC-H lite golden plans enforce cost ceilings.
Telemetry search.pruned_groups; search.tasks_executed histogram; search.budget_hit.
Recovery Reference disagreement → bound-tightening bug; reset affected query to exhaustive mode until fixed.
Debug QUASAR_SEARCH_TRACE=1 logs every task push/pop, pruning decision, cost comparison.

I8 — Generic Plan Validity Across Placeholder Bindings

Field Detail
Statement Cached generic plan (quasar-cache, Phase 13) produced without specific placeholder values is a valid physical plan for ALL type-compatible bindings.
Why Optimal strategy may vary by value (selective literal → index scan; low-selectivity → full scan); generic plan must be conservative — safe for any binding.
Enforced Generic plan generated with placeholder cardinality = neutral estimate (table rows × default_selectivity); quasar-constraint produces placeholder-aware spans (not value-specific).
Detection proptest: per prepared statement, 100 random typed bindings, verify generic-plan output semantics vs no-cache reference; quasar opt-test placeholder directive with multiple binding sets.
Telemetry cache.generic_plan_fallback (runtime decides custom plan needed).
Recovery Binding producing incorrect results → fall back to custom_plan(binding) for that invocation; increment cache.custom_plan_forced.
Debug quasar explain --opt "$sql" --placeholder-values shows per-binding plan; compare vs generic.

I9 — Memo Immutability Post Winner Extraction

Field Detail
Statement After Optimizer::extract_plan() consumes the memo, no further Memo mutation possible — memo sealed.
Why Plan extraction traverses winner pointers valid only if memo stable; post-extraction mutation invalidates ExprId refs in plan tree.
Enforced Rust ownership: extract_plan(self) -> PhysicalPlan consumes Optimizer (thus Memo); no &mut Memo after. quasar-cache stores only FlowSpec (no memo ref); memo dropped.
Detection Static — borrow checker; no runtime detection.
Telemetry N/A (compile-time invariant).
Recovery N/A — violation is a compile error.
Debug Future post-extraction query: re-optimize from fresh memo using cached FlowSpec for warm-start hints.

I10 — Determinism

Field Detail
Statement Identical (sql_text, catalog_snapshot, statistics_snapshot, optimizer_seed) → byte-identical FlowSpec on every invocation, any conforming platform.
Why Non-determinism → unreliable test goldens, impossible replay, ambiguous cost-regression attribution.
Enforced quasar-memo: no HashMap iteration in plan-affecting decisions — iterate sorted Vecs/indexed arenas. quasar-search: task stack Vec, equal-cost tie-break by ExprId. quasar-cost: deterministic IEEE 754 f64, no platform-varying FMA reorder. quasar-optgen: rule dispatch table alphabetical by name. RNG (fuzz/bench only): seed from caller.
Detection quasar-obs replay: quasar opt-test --replay <event-log> re-runs + diffs output; CI runs conformance twice with identical input, diffs plan bytes.
Telemetry replay.divergence (always zero when healthy).
Recovery Divergence → HashMap-order dependency or platform FP diff; bisect via event log to first diverging task.
Debug QUASAR_DETERMINISM_CHECK=1 runs each optimization twice internally, panics on divergence (dev only).

I11 — FD Closure Consistency

Field Detail
Statement Per group FuncDepSet: if key ⊆ out_cols, then closure(key) ⊇ out_cols (key determines all output cols); equivalency groups transitively closed; constant cols appear in constants and in all FD RHS referencing them.
Why Incorrect FDs → wrong join-order rewrites (false FD eliminates necessary join), incorrect distinct-count estimates, spurious ordering simplifications.
Enforced FuncDepSet::close() called after each per-operator FD derivation; transitivity closure via iterative fixed-point.
Detection debug_assert!(fds.closure(fds.key.as_ref().unwrap()).is_superset(out_cols)); per-operator FD unit tests: nullable FK joins, self-joins, constants from WHERE col = const.
Telemetry props.fd_closure_fixpoint_iterations histogram (P99 < 5 for well-structured queries).
Recovery Violated closure = derivation logic error → log + drop inconsistent FD rather than propagate downstream.
Debug quasar explain --opt emits FD sets (verbose); quasar memo shows per-group FDs.

I12 — Root Winner Distribution Matches Required

Field Detail
Statement Root winner provided.distribution satisfies required.distribution; if caller requires Single(r) (gateway/single-region), root is natively Single(r) or a Distribute enforcer to Single(r) is inserted.
Why Distributed plan returned when single-region required sends results to wrong node → data loss / correctness error at exec boundary.
Enforced EnforceProps inserts Distribute{input, distribution: required} when best member provides Any/different set; extract_plan() final assert winner.provided.distribution.satisfies(required.distribution).
Detection Assertion in extract_plan (debug + release); conformance test: multi-region queries require Single(gateway_region), golden plans verified to have top-level Distribute when needed.
Telemetry search.distribute_enforcer_inserted; distsql.root_distribution_mismatch (always zero; non-zero is critical alert).
Recovery No Distribute enforcer can satisfy (no route to region) → OptError::NoPlanFound naming unsatisfiable distribution.
Debug quasar explain --distsql shows top-level router; verify ByRange/PassThrough to required region node.

8. Domain Model / Source Language / Protocol

Normative reference for every artifact Quasar consumes/produces. All phases derive schemas, grammars, data flows from here.

8.1 Supported SQL Subset — EBNF

query        ::= select_stmt | insert_stmt | update_stmt | delete_stmt
select_stmt  ::= SELECT proj_list FROM table_refs
                 [WHERE expr]
                 [GROUP BY group_list [HAVING expr]]
                 [ORDER BY order_list]
                 [LIMIT expr [OFFSET expr]]

proj_list    ::= STAR | proj_item (',' proj_item)*
proj_item    ::= expr [AS ident] | table '.' STAR

table_refs   ::= table_ref (join_clause)*
table_ref    ::= table_name [AS ident] | '(' select_stmt ')' [AS ident]
join_clause  ::= [join_type] JOIN table_ref ON expr
join_type    ::= INNER | LEFT [OUTER] | SEMI | ANTI
                 (* CROSS JOIN = INNER with no ON predicate *)

group_list   ::= expr (',' expr)*
order_list   ::= order_item (',' order_item)*
order_item   ::= expr [ASC | DESC] [NULLS FIRST | NULLS LAST]

insert_stmt  ::= INSERT INTO table_name ['(' col_list ')']
                 (VALUES '(' expr_list ')' (',' '(' expr_list ')')* | select_stmt)
update_stmt  ::= UPDATE table_name SET assign_list [WHERE expr]
assign_list  ::= assign (',' assign)*
assign       ::= col_name '=' expr
delete_stmt  ::= DELETE FROM table_name [WHERE expr]

expr         ::= literal | placeholder | col_ref | func_call
               | expr bin_op expr | unary_op expr
               | expr IS [NOT] NULL | expr [NOT] IN '(' expr_list | select_stmt ')'
               | EXISTS '(' select_stmt ')' | expr cmp_op ANY | ALL '(' select_stmt ')'
               | CASE [expr] (WHEN expr THEN expr)+ [ELSE expr] END
               | COALESCE '(' expr_list ')' | CAST '(' expr AS type ')'
               | '(' select_stmt ')'       (* scalar subquery *)
               | '(' expr ')'

bin_op       ::= '+' | '-' | '*' | '/' | '%' | '||'
               | '=' | '<>' | '<' | '>' | '<=' | '>=' | LIKE | ILIKE | SIMILAR TO
               | AND | OR
unary_op     ::= '-' | NOT
cmp_op       ::= '=' | '<>' | '<' | '>' | '<=' | '>='

col_ref      ::= [table_name '.'] col_name
func_call    ::= func_name '(' [DISTINCT] expr_list ')'   (* incl. aggregate fns *)
placeholder  ::= '$' uint                                  (* 1-indexed *)

literal      ::= int_lit | float_lit | string_lit | bool_lit | NULL
type         ::= INT | BIGINT | FLOAT | DECIMAL | TEXT | BOOL | TIMESTAMP | DATE | JSONB | BYTES

Not supported (deferred/out of scope): DDL, WINDOW (Phase 13 ext), LATERAL, TABLESAMPLE, CTEs (WITH), RETURNING, MERGE. Full grammar: project 259/313.

8.2 Relational Algebra Operator Table

Operator Inputs Output Columns LogicalProp Effects
Scan(ScanOp) ∅ ScanOp.cols from table schema cardinality = table row count; keys from PK; FDs from schema
IndexScan(IndexScanOp) ∅ IndexScanOp.cols cardinality = selectivity × table rows; ordering = index key order
Select{input, filters} {input} input.out_cols cardinality reduced by selectivity(filters); FDs preserved
Project{input, projections, passthrough} {input} projected + passthrough cols FDs narrowed to surviving cols; keys only if key cols survive
InnerJoin(JoinOp) {left, right} left.out_cols ∪ right.out_cols cardinality = left × right × selectivity(on); FK join may preserve key
LeftJoin(JoinOp) {left, right} left.out_cols ∪ right.out_cols cardinality ≥ left.rows; right cols nullable
SemiJoin(JoinOp) {left, right} left.out_cols cardinality ≤ left.rows; left duplicates preserved
AntiJoin(JoinOp) {left, right} left.out_cols cardinality ≤ left.rows
HashJoin(JoinOp) {build, probe} same as InnerJoin physical: requires no ordering; provides Any distribution
MergeJoin(MergeJoinOp) {left, right} same as InnerJoin physical: requires ordered input on join keys; provides ordered output
LookupJoin(LookupJoinOp) {input} input.out_cols ∪ index.cols physical: preserves input ordering; often Single distribution
InvertedJoin {input} input.out_cols ∪ table.cols physical: JSONB / geo inverted index
ZigzagJoin {idx1, idx2} intersection of key cols physical: two-pointer traversal over sorted index scans
GroupBy{input, grouping_cols, aggs} {input} grouping_cols ∪ agg_result_cols key = grouping_cols (if unique); cardinality ≤ NDV(grouping_cols)
ScalarGroupBy{input, aggs} {input} agg_result_cols cardinality = 1
Distinct{input, cols} {input} input.out_cols cardinality ≤ NDV(cols)
Sort{input, ordering} {input} input.out_cols provides ordering; enforcer node
Limit{input, n, offset} {input} input.out_cols cardinality = min(input.card, n)
UnionAll {left, right} left.out_cols cardinality = left.rows + right.rows
Union {left, right} left.out_cols cardinality ≤ left.rows + right.rows; dedup
Intersect / Except {left, right} left.out_cols cardinality ≤ min/left rows
Values ∅ declared cols cardinality = literal row count
WithScan{id} ∅ CTE cols cardinality = CTE's stats
Distribute{input, distribution} {input} input.out_cols enforcer: provides requested distribution; net cost added
SortEnforcer{input, ordering} {input} input.out_cols enforcer: provides ordering; CPU sort cost
Insert / Update / Delete / Upsert {input, table} mutation result cardinality = affected rows
Window {input} input.out_cols ∪ window_cols Phase 13 extension

8.3 Scalar Expression Grammar (Type System)

scalar_expr  ::= Column(ColumnId)                          (* bound col reference *)
               | Const(Datum)                              (* typed literal *)
               | Placeholder(PlaceholderIdx)               (* $N, 1-indexed *)
               | And(Vec<ScalarId>)                        (* short-circuit; ≥ 2 *)
               | Or(Vec<ScalarId>)
               | Not(Box<ScalarId>)
               | Comparison { op: CmpOp, l: ScalarId, r: ScalarId }
               | BinOp { op: BinOpKind, l: ScalarId, r: ScalarId }
               | UnaryOp { op: UnaryOpKind, expr: ScalarId }
               | Function { id: FuncId, args: Vec<ScalarId> }
               | Cast { expr: ScalarId, typ: DataType }
               | Case { base: Option<ScalarId>, when_then: Vec<(ScalarId,ScalarId)>, else_: Option<ScalarId> }
               | Coalesce(Vec<ScalarId>)
               | Tuple(Vec<ScalarId>)
               | Array(Vec<ScalarId>)
               | Subquery(GroupId)                         (* scalar subquery -> group returns 1 row, 1 col *)
               | Exists(GroupId)
               | AnyAll { cmp: CmpOp, scalar: ScalarId, sub: GroupId }

datum        ::= Int(i64) | Float(f64) | Decimal(Decimal128) | Text(Arc<str>)
               | Bool(bool) | Null | Bytes(Bytes) | Timestamp(i64) | Date(i32)
               | JsonB(Arc<Value>) | Array(Arc<[Datum]>) | Tuple(Arc<[Datum]>)

Type checking: quasar-bind resolves every ScalarExpr to a DataType; type errors → OptError::Unsupported (human-readable). Implicit casts follow CockroachDB promotion rules (int→float, int→decimal, …).

8.4 Bound IR (RelExpr / ScalarExpr) from Spine

SPINE definitions normative. Constraints imposed by quasar-bind:

  • Every ColumnId globally unique within a query; allocated monotonically from a per-query counter.
  • Subqueries = GroupId refs into memo; correlated columns appear as outer_cols in referenced group's LogicalProps.
  • ScanOp.cols/IndexScanOp.cols = minimal ColSet needed (projection pushdown applied by binder).
  • GroupBy aggregation inputs as ScalarId in aggregations; result cols are fresh binder-allocated ColumnIds.

8.5 Worked Example: End-to-End (3-Table Join + Filter + Group-By)

SQL:

SELECT c.region, COUNT(*) AS orders
FROM customer c
JOIN orders o ON c.id = o.customer_id
JOIN order_item i ON o.id = i.order_id
WHERE i.amount > 100
GROUP BY c.region
ORDER BY orders DESC
LIMIT 10;

Step 1 — quasar-sql: raw AST (abbreviated)

SelectStmt {
  proj: [Column("c.region"), FuncCall("COUNT", [Star])],
  from: Join(Join(Table("customer","c"), Table("orders","o"), ON c.id=o.customer_id),
              Table("order_item","i"), ON o.id=i.order_id),
  where: Comparison(>,Column("i.amount"),Lit(100)),
  group_by: [Column("c.region")], order_by: [Desc("orders")], limit: Lit(10)
}

Step 2 — quasar-bind: Bound RelExpr tree

Sort [col7 DESC]
  Limit [n=10]
    Project [col5 (c.region), col6 (COUNT)]
      GroupBy [grouping=col5] [agg: COUNT(*)->col6]
        Select [col4 > 100]                    ; i.amount
          InnerJoin [col2 = col3]              ; o.id = i.order_id
            InnerJoin [col0 = col1]            ; c.id = o.customer_id
              Scan(customer, cols={col0,col5}) ; id, region
              Scan(orders,   cols={col1,col2}) ; customer_id, id
            Scan(order_item, cols={col3,col4}) ; order_id, amount

ColumnIds: col0=customer.id, col1=orders.customer_id, col2=orders.id, col3=order_item.order_id, col4=order_item.amount, col5=customer.region, col6=COUNT result.

Step 3 — quasar-memo: Initial memo (logical groups)

GroupId Operator Children LogicalProps (cardinality)
G0 Scan(customer) — rows≈1M, keys={col0}
G1 Scan(orders) — rows≈5M, keys={col2}
G2 InnerJoin [col0=col1] G0,G1 rows≈5M
G3 Scan(order_item) — rows≈20M, keys={PK}
G4 InnerJoin [col2=col3] G2,G3 rows≈20M
G5 Select [col4>100] G4 rows≈2M (sel≈0.1)
G6 GroupBy [col5] G5 rows≈200 (NDV(region))
G7 Project [col5,col6] G6 rows=200
G8 Limit [10] G7 rows=10
G9 Sort [col6 DESC] G8 rows=10

Step 4 — Normalization (on-construct)

  • Select [col4>100] pushed below InnerJoin [col2=col3] → Select on G3 (Scan order_item); new group G3' = Select{G3, col4>100}, rows≈2M.
  • Two InnerJoin predicate-merge: no merge (different predicates).
  • Sort + Limit → single Sort-with-limit-hint on G8 (limit pushdown into ordering).

Step 5 — Exploration (during search, Phase 8) — alternatives for G4 (join of G2, G3'):

Alternative Rule Notes
InnerJoin(G3',G2) JoinCommutativity reorder operands
InnerJoin(G0, InnerJoin(G1,G3')) JoinReassociate different tree shape
InnerJoin(G2, G3') GenerateIndexScan G3' may use IndexScan on order_item(order_id)

Step 6 — Implementation (Phase 9, chosen plan)

chosen physical plan (post search, costs in (cpu, io, net)):
Sort [col6 DESC] limit=10        cost=(0.01, 0, 0)
  HashAgg [col5] -> col6         cost=(0.5, 0, 0)    ; StreamAgg rejected (no ordering)
    LookupJoin [col2=col3]       cost=(0.2, 1.2, 0)  ; order_item PK lookup from orders
      MergeJoin [col0=col1]      cost=(0.3, 0, 0)    ; c.id=o.customer_id, both indexed
        IndexScan(customer PK)   cost=(0.1, 0.5, 0)
        IndexScan(orders FK idx) cost=(0.1, 0.8, 0)
      Filter [col4>100]                               ; pushed into LookupJoin post-filter

Step 7 — DistSQL FlowSpec (Phase 12, single-region)

FlowSpec:
  P0: TableReader{customer PK scan}  @ node-1
  P1: TableReader{orders FK idx}     @ node-1
  P2: MergeJoiner{col0=col1}         @ node-1   <-- stream P0,P1
  P3: TableReader{order_item lookup} @ node-2   <-- lookup key from P2
  P4: HashJoiner{col2=col3}          @ gateway  <-- stream P2 (hash col2), P3 (hash col3)
  P5: Aggregator{HashAgg col5}       @ gateway
  P6: Sorter{col6 DESC, limit=10}    @ gateway
Streams: P0->P2, P1->P2, P2->P4(ByHash col2), P3->P4(ByHash col3), P4->P5, P5->P6

8.6 Index Constraint Example

Schema. events(a INT, b INT, c TEXT), index idx_ab(a ASC, b ASC). Predicate. WHERE a = 5 AND b > 10 AND c = 'ok'

Constraint derivation (quasar-constraint):

Index key cols: [a, b]
Predicate analysis:
  a = 5        -> exact match on col 0  -> start=[5,⊥], end=[5,∞]  (equality prefix)
  b > 10       -> range on col 1        -> start=(5,10], end=[5,∞)  (exclusive start on b)
  c = 'ok'     -> not an index col      -> remaining filter

Constraint {
  cols: [col_a, col_b],
  spans: [Span { start: Key[5,10], start_boundary: Exclusive,
                  end:   Key[5],   end_boundary:   Exclusive /* open */ }],
  tight: false   (* remaining filter: c = 'ok' not covered *)
}
remaining_filter: ScalarExpr::Comparison { op: Eq, l: Column(col_c), r: Const("ok") }

IN expansion. WHERE a IN (3, 7) AND b > 10:

Spans: [Span{[3,10]excl .. [3]open}, Span{[7,10]excl .. [7]open}]  tight: false

Partial index. idx_partial(a) WHERE status = 'active': applicable only when query WHERE includes status = 'active' (quasar-constraint partial-index filter matcher). Inverted index. WHERE jsonb_col @> '{"key":"val"}': constraint builder extracts inverted span from containment predicate; remaining filter re-checks exact match.

8.7 Distribution and Locality Model

Regions / Ranges / Leaseholders. CockroachDB-style cluster partitions table data into Ranges (RangeId); each range has a leaseholder on a NodeId in a RegionId. quasar-cat accessors (signatures):

fn leaseholder_node(range: RangeId) -> NodeId;
fn node_region(node: NodeId) -> RegionId;
fn ranges_for_span(table: TableId, index: IndexId, span: &Span) -> Vec<RangeId>;

Distribution enum:

Distribution::Any          -- data may be anywhere; optimizer chooses placement
Distribution::Single(r)    -- all rows at gateway region r (final result)
Distribution::Set(regions) -- data partitioned across this region set (distributed join)

Distribute enforcer needed when:

  • Root always requires Single(gateway_region).
  • HashJoin hashing both sides by join key: each side needs Set(all_nodes) + ByHash router.
  • MergeJoin: both sides need matching ordering AND distribution to avoid cross-region streaming.
  • Local-only plan (all ranges in gateway region): distribution already Single; no enforcer.

Locality-optimized search (LOS): join build side all-local (gateway region) → prefer LookupJoin over HashJoin even if cardinality favors hash, because net cost dominates. LOS = Phase 11 net-cost uplift.

Distribution derivation per operator:

Operator Provided Distribution
Scan / IndexScan Set(regions_of_ranges(table, spans))
LookupJoin same as input (input drives, lookups co-located)
HashJoin Set(all_nodes) after hash routing both sides
MergeJoin same as inputs (must match; enforcer inserts if not)
Aggregator (HashAgg) Any → placed at gateway after partial agg
Distribute enforcer exactly the requested distribution
Values Single(gateway)

8.8 Search Task State Machine

Cascades search runs a task stack. Initial: stack = [OptimizeGroup(root, required_props)].

Task Pre-conditions Actions Posts tasks
OptimizeGroup(g, req) g not optimized check winners, compute cost bound OptimizeExpr(e, req) ∀ e ∈ g.members
g has winner prune if winner.cost ≥ budget (none)
OptimizeExpr(e, req) e physical cost e; update winner if better OptimizeGroup(child, child_req) ∀ child
e logical skip (logical not directly costed) ExploreExpr(e) if not explored
ExploreGroup(g) g.explored == false set g.explored = true ExploreExpr(e) ∀ e ∈ g.members
ExploreExpr(e) e logical for each applicable explore rule ApplyRule(e, r) ∀ unapplied rule
e physical (no explore rules apply) (none)
ApplyRule(e, r) r ∉ e.applied fire rule; intern new MExpr(s); set e.rules_applied.set(r) ExploreExpr/OptimizeExpr(new_e)
EnforceProps(g, req) no winner satisfies req create enforcer MExpr (Sort/Dist) OptimizeExpr(enforcer_e, req)
winner exists (no-op) (none)

Termination. Stack empty → complete. Budget exceeded → return current best winner or NoPlanFound.

Explore vs Optimize phase: Explore = only logical→logical rules fire, no costing. Optimize = implementation rules fire (logical→physical), physical exprs costed, winners updated.

State diagram (simplified):

                      ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
                      │  OptimizeGroup                           │
                      │  ┌──has winner──┐                        │
                      │  │  prune?      │                        │
                      │  └──push Exprs──┘                        │
                      └───────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                                  │ for each member
                      ┌───────────▼──────────────────────────────┐
                      │  OptimizeExpr                            │
                      │  physical? → cost → update winner        │
                      │  logical?  → ExploreExpr                 │
                      └───────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
         ┌──────────────────────┐ │             ┌──────────────────────────┐
         │ ExploreGroup         │◄┘             │ EnforceProps             │
         │ → ExploreExpr(each)  │               │ insert Sort/Distribute   │
         └──────────┬───────────┘               └──────────────────────────┘
                    │
         ┌──────────▼───────────┐
         │ ApplyRule(e, r)      │
         │ → new MExpr → intern │
         └──────────────────────┘

8.9 Failure-Path Examples

8.9.1 Unsupported SQL

Input:  SELECT * FROM t1 FULL OUTER JOIN t2 ON t1.id = t2.id
Result: OptError::Unsupported("FULL OUTER JOIN not in supported operator set")
EXPLAIN: (none; error returned before memo construction)

8.9.2 Missing Statistics

Input:  SELECT * FROM new_table WHERE col = 1  (stats never collected)
Effect: quasar-stats returns Statistics { row_count: DEFAULT_ROW_COUNT (1000),
          col_stats: {col → ColumnStatistic { distinct_count: DEFAULT_NDV,
          null_count: 0, histogram: None, avg_size: 8 }}, is_estimated: true }
Plan:   proceeds; may be sub-optimal (no histogram → uniform selectivity)
Warn:   metric stats.estimated_fallback incremented; EXPLAIN shows "estimated (no stats)"

8.9.3 Unsatisfiable Constraint

Input:  WHERE a > 10 AND a < 5 over index on a
Solver: Span{(10,∞)} ∩ Span{(-∞,5)} = ∅  (empty intersection)
Result: Constraint { spans: Spans::empty(), tight: true }
Plan:   IndexScan empty spans → zero rows; optimizer prunes to Values(empty)
        via normalization rule EmptySpanElimination → RelExpr::Values(rows=0)

8.9.4 Unbound Placeholder

Input:  quasar opt "SELECT * FROM t WHERE id = $1"  (no binding provided)
Mode:   single-shot (not prepared statement path)
Result: OptError::PlaceholderUnbound(PlaceholderIdx(0))
        message: "placeholder $1 has no binding and no generic-plan mode active"

8.9.5 Budget Exceeded

Input:  50-table join (complex star schema)
Effect: budget (Cost::default_budget()) exhausted before full exploration
Result: OptError::BudgetExceeded(actual_cost_spent)
        OR: best plan so far returned with warning header in EXPLAIN
Note:   quasar-cache can store partial-plan result; next call w/ same fingerprint may reuse

8.9.6 Catalog Missing

Input:  SELECT * FROM nonexistent_table
Bind:   quasar-bind calls catalog.resolve_table("nonexistent_table") → None
Result: OptError::CatalogMissing("table 'nonexistent_table' not found")
        returned before memo construction

8.10 Sequence Diagram: Full Optimize() Call

  caller           quasar-sql    quasar-bind   quasar-memo     quasar-search   quasar-distsql
    |                   |              |              |                |               |
    |--- parse(sql) --->|              |              |                |               |
    |<-- raw AST -------|              |              |                |               |
    |--- bind(ast,cat) ------------->  |              |                |               |
    |          catalog lookups ------> cat            |                |               |
    |<-- bound RelExpr/ScalarExpr ---|              |                |               |
    |--- build_memo(bound_expr) ------------------> |                |               |
    |          normalize-on-construct fires          |                |               |
    |          logical props derived                 |                |               |
    |<-- Memo (root GroupId) ---------------------- |                |               |
    |--- optimize(memo, required_props) ------------------------>    |               |
    |          task stack executes (Explore / Implement / Cost)     |               |
    |          winners memoized per (group, phys_props)             |               |
    |<-- PhysicalPlan (winner tree) -------------------------------- |               |
    |--- build_flow(physical_plan, catalog) ------------------------------------->  |
    |          node placement by leaseholder                                        |
    |<-- FlowSpec ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
    |
    | (return FlowSpec to caller / EXPLAIN rendered)

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

All decisions below are locked for the blueprint phase. Rationale is one line; rejected alternatives each carry a brief why-not.

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
A01 Implementation language Rust (stable, 2021 edition) Go — GC pauses hurt optimize() tail latency; weaker sum types make rule match non-exhaustive. C++ — memory-unsafe for a shared DAG with many u32 handles. OCaml/Haskell — excellent ADTs; weaker ops/ecosystem for this portfolio. Algebraic enums + borrow checker enforce memo append-mostly + owner model at compile time; no GC pauses → predictable latency. Go wins when the optimizer must be embedded into a Go-dominant monorepo (as CockroachDB itself demonstrates); the GC pauses are tolerable when latency SLOs are >10 ms and the organizational cost of a Rust FFI boundary exceeds the performance gain.
A02 Rust edition + toolchain pin Edition 2021; rust-toolchain.toml with exact nightly-free stable channel pin Nightly — would enable allocator_api, not needed; unstable APIs break reproducibility. Stable without pin — dependency upgrades change MSRV silently. Reproducible CI; edition 2021 gives let … else, pattern closures, and improved borrow scopes needed for arena traversal. Nightly wins when allocator_api or specialization are required for the arena allocator hot path and the team accepts regular nightly-breakage triage overhead.
A03 Build system cargo workspace with 16 member crates under crates/ Bazel — full Bazel porting overhead unjustified for a single-org project. CMake — not Rust-native. Meson — N/A. Cargo workspace provides incremental builds, per-crate unit tests, and the build.rs hook needed for optgen codegen without extra tooling. Bazel wins in a monorepo with multiple languages (Rust + C++ + Python analytics) where cross-language dependency caching and hermetic remote execution justify the setup cost; Google's F1 query layer exemplifies this.
A04 Rule engine optgen .opt DSL compiled at build time via build.rs in quasar-optgen → generated Rust match/replace code in quasar-rules Runtime interpreter — no compile-time exhaustiveness; slower; harder to test. Declarative macro (macro_rules!) — unreadable for 200+ rules; poor IDE support. Proc-macro — possible but heavier compile overhead vs build.rs. CockroachDB's optgen approach: rules are a separate, readable DSL; generated Rust is fast (no interpreter overhead); build.rs invalidation tracks .opt file changes automatically. A runtime interpreter wins in systems that require hot-reload of rules without recompilation — e.g., a SaaS optimizer where rule updates must propagate to a running fleet in seconds, or a research optimizer where rules change every iteration of an experiment.
A05 Memo representation Typed arena (Arena<Group>, Arena<MExpr>) + u32 newtype handles (GroupId, ExprId) + HashMap<MExprKey, ExprId> intern table Rc<RefCell<…>> graph — non-deterministic drop order; GC-like cycle risk; no safe cross-thread serialization. petgraph — generic; no structural dedup built in; GroupId/ExprId would need to be petgraph NodeIndex which is usize, losing newtype safety. Index-into-Vec — same as arena but without the Arena abstraction for bounds-checking. Pointer-free handles: safe to clone, hash, serialize for replay; arena allocation is cache-friendly; structural sharing via intern table is a O(1) lookup. Rc<RefCell> wins in a prototype or interactive REPL where GC semantics simplify rule authoring and memo sharing; for a research optimizer running fewer than 100-group queries where latency is not a constraint, the cognitive simplicity outweighs allocation overhead.
A06 Search strategy Cascades top-down branch-and-bound (Graefe 1995 / Orca lineage) Volcano bottom-up — does not naturally support goal-directed search (optimizing for specific physical properties); cost pruning harder. System-R DP join enumeration — fixed join-order DP, not rule-based; cannot express arbitrary transformations via .opt rules. Separate DPccp for join ordering — DPccp is optimal for join ordering but requires a separate phase and integration with the rule framework; transformation-rule enumeration (JoinCommutativity + JoinAssociate + JoinReassociate) integrates naturally into the existing Cascades ApplyRule task and interleaves with other rules. Cascades unifies exploration and implementation in one task-driven engine; B&B pruning avoids full enumeration in the common case; goal-directed search naturally handles physical properties and multi-region distribution requirements. System-R bottom-up DP wins when the query workload is strictly join-order-dominated (star schemas, ≤12 tables, all joins inner equi-joins) and the optimizer must be embedded in a resource-constrained environment where Cascades' task-stack overhead and memo memory are prohibitive — PostgreSQL's planner successfully uses this approach for the vast majority of OLTP workloads.
A07 Join-order enumeration Transformation rules (JoinCommutativity, JoinAssociate, JoinReassociate) within Cascades search Standalone DPccp phase (Moerkotte & Neumann 2006) — adds complexity; doesn't compose with other rule types; would require extracting the join graph before Cascades and re-merging results. Greedy heuristic — non-optimal; not suitable for a cost-based optimizer at this quality bar. Transformation rules enumerate the same plans as DPccp for inner joins when fully applied; they compose with predicate pushdown, index scan generation, and distribution rules in a single search pass. DPccp wins when the optimizer handles complex join graphs with cross-products and non-inner joins (outer/semi/anti) that transformation rules struggle to enumerate efficiently; HyPer/DuckDB use DPhyp precisely for these cases, achieving exhaustive optimal join ordering for queries that rule-based enumeration under-explores.
A08 Cost representation struct Cost { cpu: f64, io: f64, net: f64, total: f64 } Single f64 scalar — loses breakdown needed for distribution-aware tuning (net cost dominates in multi-region; io cost dominates in local scan-heavy plans). Integer cost units (CBO classic) — requires calibration to a fixed unit; f64 allows direct multiplication by row counts and selectivity without overflow. Three-component cost makes the Coster implementation readable and testable per component; total is the B&B comparison key and can be tuned (e.g., total = cpu + io + w_net * net) without changing the struct. A single f64 wins in a single-node optimizer where all queries run locally and network cost is identically zero; the extra fields add struct size and Coster complexity for no discriminating benefit — DuckDB's cost model is effectively single-dimensional for exactly this reason.
A09 NDV estimation HyperLogLog sketch (HLL++) offline → ColumnStatistic.distinct_count: f64 Exact COUNT(DISTINCT col) at plan time — too expensive; requires full table scan. KMV sketch — lower accuracy per byte than HLL++. Sampling-based NDV — biased for skewed distributions. HLL++ achieves 0.5–2% relative error at 16KB per column; offline collection (ANALYZE equivalent) matches CockroachDB's stats model; f64 for distinct_count allows fractional estimates for partial stats. Sampling-based NDV wins for append-only analytical tables where exact HLL sketches are impractical to maintain and a 5–10% NDV error is acceptable — Amazon Redshift and Snowflake use adaptive sampling in their auto-ANALYZE pipelines for this reason.
A10 Histograms Equi-depth (equal-frequency) histograms, Bucket { upper_bound, num_eq, num_range, distinct_range } Equi-width — poor resolution for skewed data (frequent values get the same bucket width as sparse regions). Max-diff (Ioannidis-Kang) — optimal for skewed data but complex maintenance; overkill for the 100-bucket default. V-optimal — requires sorting and dynamic programming to build; more expensive than equi-depth. Equi-depth provides accurate range selectivity for skewed data; the num_eq / num_range / distinct_range per-bucket model (CockroachDB's Histogram format) supports equality and range predicates. Max-diff histograms win when the column value distribution is highly skewed with a few extremely frequent values driving most query plans — e.g., a status column where 99% of rows are 'active'; equi-depth merges rare values into wide buckets with poor selectivity, while max-diff places bucket boundaries at exactly the discontinuities that matter.
A11 Multi-column statistics Multi-column ColumnStatistic keyed by ColSet (up to 4-column combos) Full joint distribution — exponential storage. Independence assumption always — inaccurate for correlated columns (e.g., city→zip_code). Foreign-key correlation inference only — too narrow. Multi-column stats for common join/group-by column sets captures the most important correlations; collected on-demand (ANALYZE with column list); stored in Statistics.col_stats: HashMap<ColSet, ColumnStatistic>. Full joint distribution wins for a small-schema OLAP system (≤10 columns, ≤5 query templates) where the exponential storage is affordable and the correlation between all column pairs is the dominant source of plan instability — as demonstrated by academic systems like IQ (Ilyas et al.) that use full-join-distribution sketches.
A12 Selectivity model Per-predicate: equality uses 1/NDV; range uses histogram bucket interpolation; AND chains multiply selectivities (independence); OR uses inclusion-exclusion (2-term); IN uses len(in_list) / NDV MCV (most common values) table — maintained separately; adds storage; integrated in histograms via num_eq. Sampling at plan time — too expensive. Learned selectivity (ML model) — deferred; not stable for a foundational optimizer. CockroachDB's model: histogram-backed range selectivity + NDV-based equality + independence assumption for conjuncts is accurate enough for TPC-H/TPC-DS workloads; simpler to reason about and test. A learned ML selectivity model wins in systems with stable, high-query-volume workloads where historical feedback can train a neural or gradient-boosted estimator — Bao (Marcus et al. 2021) and Neo show that learned estimators reduce median q-error by 2–5× over histogram-based approaches on JOB-style benchmarks with complex multi-predicate correlation.
A13 Constraint solver design Dedicated quasar-constraint crate: predicate analysis → Constraint { cols, spans, tight } + remaining filter Inline in the index scan rule — hard to unit-test independently; couples rule logic with span math. External SMT solver — extreme overkill; adds a heavy dependency. Ad-hoc per-operator — no reuse between IndexScan and LookupJoin key constraint derivation. Separating constraint solving allows independent unit tests for span arithmetic (intersection, union, inversion); reused by IndexScan rule, LookupJoin key extraction, and ZigzagJoin. An external SMT solver (e.g., Z3) wins when the predicate language includes disjunctive normal form constraints over computed columns, partial-index predicates with arbitrary arithmetic, or user-defined type constraints — scenarios where exact span derivation is undecidable or too complex to enumerate manually.
A14 Physical-property framework PhysProps { ordering: OrderingChoice, distribution: Distribution, limit_hint: f64 } with FD-simplification of OrderingChoice Property templates (Volcano style) — less composable; harder to extend for Distribution property. Separate required/provided type variants — adds boilerplate; same information. PhysProps as a value type: hashable, comparable, cloneable; serves as the key in Group.winners: HashMap<PhysProps, Winner>; FD-simplification reduces the number of distinct required-props keys, limiting winner table size. Volcano-style property templates win when the property space is extremely large and heterogeneous — e.g., a multi-model database with document, graph, and relational operators each having incompatible property types that cannot be unified into a single struct without excessive padding.
A15 Distribution property modeling `Distribution::Any Single(RegionId) Set(SmallVec<[RegionId;4]>)` Bit-vector over all nodes — too large; most plans involve ≤ 4 regions. Always-distributed model (ignore locality) — misses the LOS optimization that is Quasar's core value proposition in multi-region settings.
A16 DistSQL flow serialization Rust-native FlowSpec struct (in-process; no protobuf for the optimizer's internal boundary) Protocol Buffers — necessary for wire-protocol to an execution engine; but the boundary between Quasar and exec (project 318) is in-process in this portfolio. JSON/serde — too verbose for large flows; acceptable only for EXPLAIN output. FlowSpec is passed by value to the downstream exec crate; serde + JSON is used only for EXPLAIN (DISTSQL) rendering and for replay event logs. If a network boundary is later needed, protobuf derives from the same struct. Protobuf wins the moment the optimizer and executor run in separate processes or across a network (e.g., a centralized query planning service serving multiple executor clusters) — Impala's statestore-and-planner architecture uses exactly this split, with Thrift/Protobuf as the plan-distribution wire format.
A17 Determinism strategy Sorted iteration over arenas; ExprId-stable winner tie-breaking; no HashMap iteration in plan-affecting hot paths; seeded RNG in Optimizer struct Non-deterministic iteration with sorted output at the end — sorting is a partial fix; any mid-pipeline branch on HashMap order is still non-deterministic. Thread-local RNG — would vary per thread; optimizer is single-threaded per query. Determinism is required for golden tests, replay, and cost-regression attribution (I10). A seeded u64 field in Optimizer allows reproducible bench/fuzz runs. Non-deterministic-but-sorted wins in an optimizer where plan quality is evaluated statistically (A/B test over thousands of queries) rather than by exact regression; the BTreeMap overhead is measurable at 50k-group memos and the golden-test framework uses cost-range comparisons rather than byte-identical plan text.
A18 Concurrency model Single-threaded Optimizer per query (no Sync on Memo); quasar-cat is Arc<Catalog> (read-only, Send+Sync); quasar-cache uses RwLock<PlanCache> Parallel Cascades (multi-threaded task stack) — complex; requires Arc<Mutex<Group>>; GC-like overhead; not needed for single-query optimization budgets ≤ 100ms. Actor model per group — over-engineered; data dependencies between groups require coordination. Single-threaded optimizer with per-query arena is the CockroachDB model; parallelism is achieved at the query-concurrency level (many queries, each single-threaded optimizer). Parallel Cascades wins for extremely complex analytical queries (200+ table joins, multi-hour planning acceptable, plan quality >> planning latency) — Orca/GPORCA explored this with a parallel task scheduler for Greenplum queries that could not complete under a single-threaded budget.
A19 Plan cache + placeholders quasar-cache: generic plan (no specific placeholder values) vs custom plan (specific values); cache key = (sql_fingerprint, schema_version, stats_version); invalidation on schema/stats staleness Only generic plans — may be sub-optimal for highly selective literals. Only custom plans — cache misses on every new literal set; defeats prepared-statement performance. LRU by SQL text — ignores schema/stats staleness; returns stale plans. Generic plan cached; custom plan triggered when placeholder-specific selectivity differs by >10× from generic estimate. Invalidation via schema_version + stats_version epoch counters (CockroachDB's approach). Custom-plan-only wins when prepared statements carry highly selective or correlated placeholder values (e.g., per-customer partition keys in a multi-tenant SaaS) and re-optimization cost is low relative to execution cost — PostgreSQL's plan_cache_mode = force_custom_plan is designed for exactly this scenario.
A20 Error handling enum OptError { … } (typed variants) + thiserror derive anyhow — erases error type; makes matching for recovery impossible in quasar-search. Box<dyn Error> — same erasure problem. Panic — unacceptable in a library crate. Typed errors allow callers (EXPLAIN, cache, CLI) to handle NoPlanFound vs BudgetExceeded vs CatalogMissing differently; thiserror provides Display + Error derives without boilerplate. anyhow wins in a binary or CLI application (not a library) where all errors are ultimately printed to the user and the recovery action is always "exit 1" — the call site simplicity of ? chaining without match-arm boilerplate outweighs the type-erasure cost in that context.
A21 Logging / tracing tracing crate + tracing-subscriber (structured spans per optimize() call) log crate — no structured fields; harder to correlate with a specific query. slog — less ecosystem traction than tracing; awkward async integration. Custom logger — reinventing the wheel. tracing spans map naturally to optimizer phases (bind, memo-build, search, flow-plan); span fields carry GroupId/ExprId for correlation; quasar-obs replay harness captures events via a custom tracing::Subscriber. log wins in a no-std embedded environment or a resource-constrained system where tracing's span allocation and subscriber registry add unacceptable overhead — e.g., an optimizer running inside a WebAssembly module in a browser query engine.
A22 Metrics metrics crate facade (counters/histograms/gauges) + metrics-exporter-prometheus in CLI OpenTelemetry SDK — correct long-term, but heavy; facade allows swapping exporters later. StatsD — UDP unreliable; no histogram support. Custom atomic counters — no labeling; hard to consume. metrics facade is zero-cost when no exporter is registered (optimizer library); CLI wires a Prometheus exporter for quasar bench. OpenTelemetry wins when the optimizer is one component of a broader observability platform that already standardizes on OTLP for traces, metrics, and logs — the unified context propagation across optimizer + executor + storage components eliminates the need for correlation by query ID.
A23 Testing: golden/conformance quasar opt-test datadriven runner (CockroachDB opt-tester style): SQL + directive + expected text diff Unit tests only — miss end-to-end plan quality regressions. Snapshot testing (insta) — good but no directive-based multi-output (norm vs opt vs distsql from one file). Datadriven golden files are human-readable, diff-friendly, and can encode multiple query variants in one file; the directive model (norm/explore/opt/memo/stats/index-constraints/dist) matches CockroachDB's battle-tested test infrastructure. insta snapshot testing wins for a smaller optimizer (< 20 rules, < 5 plan types) where the full directive language is over-engineered and the primary regression signal is "did the plan tree change?" — the lower setup overhead makes it preferable in early-prototype phases.
A24 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) + structure-aware Arbitrary impls on RelExpr, ScalarExpr, Memo, Cost, optimizer seeds AFL only — libFuzzer provides better coverage tracking in CI. Random string fuzzing of SQL text — misses deep internal state; most inputs fail parse early. Structure-aware fuzzing directly generates valid memo states and cost inputs, bypassing the SQL parser; finds optimizer invariant violations (I1–I12) not reachable from random SQL strings. AFL with grammar-based mutation wins when the primary attack surface is the SQL parser itself (security audit of a user-facing endpoint) rather than internal optimizer invariants — AFL's fork-server model produces higher SQL-grammar coverage per second for syntactic vulnerability research.
A25 Property testing proptest for statistical/algebraic properties (FD closure, cost monotonicity, constraint soundness, placeholder validity) QuickCheck — less expressive shrinking; proptest's strategy combinators map better to complex domain types. proptest shrinking finds minimal counterexamples for violated invariants; combined with cargo-fuzz covers both property violations and memory-safety bugs. QuickCheck wins in a Haskell or Erlang codebase where the type system's Arbitrary instances are already idiomatic and the team's toolchain familiarity reduces the effective discovery time compared to porting strategies to proptest.
A26 CI GitHub Actions: ci.yml runs cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy -- -D warnings, cargo fmt --check, cargo doc, quasar opt-test conformance/testdata/, TPC-H lite cost ceilings, cargo fuzz run (short budget) Jenkins — not hosted; overkill for a single-repo project. CircleCI — additional cost. GitHub Actions integrates with the repository; matrix over stable Rust pin; artifact upload for plan goldens and bench results. Jenkins wins in an air-gapped enterprise environment where GitHub Actions is unavailable and the team already operates a Jenkins cluster with custom hardware runners (e.g., bare-metal servers with large memory for fuzzing or cross-compilation for proprietary architectures).
A27 License Apache-2.0 MIT — less explicit patent grant (relevant for an optimizer with algorithmic novelty). GPL — incompatible with potential embedding in a commercial distribution. Apache-2.0 matches CockroachDB's optimizer (Pebble/CockroachDB Apache license); explicit patent clause protects users of the optimizer library. GPL wins if the project's strategic goal is copyleft — ensuring all derivative optimizers remain open source — rather than maximum embeddability; the FSF's position is that GPL maximizes long-term community benefit by preventing proprietary forks from withholding improvements.
A28 Dependency policy Locked Cargo.lock committed; cargo deny checks for license conflicts and known vulnerabilities; no unsafe in optimizer logic (only in arena allocator, audited separately) Unpinned deps — silent breaking changes in transitive deps. Vendoring all deps — high maintenance burden. Cargo.lock + cargo deny gives reproducible builds and security hygiene; arena's unsafe block is isolated and reviewed per-commit. Vendoring all deps wins in a regulated or air-gapped environment (financial, defense, medical device) where the supply-chain audit requires every byte of every dependency to be checked in, reviewed, and approved before build — the maintenance burden is explicitly accepted as the compliance cost.

quasar-optgen 4 .opt DSL compiler; build.rs codegen
quasar-rules 4, 8, 9 .opt sources + generated dispatch; Normalize/Explore/Implement
