Benchmarking Strategy

Level Tool Scope Location
Micro criterion (per-crate) Individual hot ops crates/*/benches/
End-to-end optimize() criterion Full pipeline per TPC-H / TPC-DS-lite query benchmarks/e2e/
Conformance throughput custom harness All conformance/testdata/ in batch benchmarks/conformance/
Memory dhat / heaptrack Peak memo bytes per query Ad hoc + CI gate

Criterion: 100 samples, warm-up 3 s, measurement 10 s; baseline JSON in benchmarks/baselines/; bench-regress compares, ±10% wall-clock → fail.

Bench targets: memo_intern (Memo::add_expr interning+dedup); props_derive (LogicalProps per operator); fd_closure (FuncDepSet::closure() realistic schemas); selectivity_point / selectivity_range (histogram); constraint_solve (solver::to_spans() multi-col index); cost_scan (Coster::cost() TableReader varying rows); search_tpch_q5 (full optimize() join-heavy); search_tpch_q18 (group-by + subquery); distsql_flow_plan (flow_plan() 8-processor query).

Profiling Methodology

1. cargo build --release --profile profiling   (opt-level=2, debug=1, no LTO)
2. perf record -g -- quasar bench tpch   |  cargo flamegraph --bench search_tpch_q5
3. perf report --symbol-filter quasar
4. cargo flamegraph -> flamegraph.svg

Expected hotspots / mitigations:

Hotspot Root cause Mitigation
Rule matching in ApplyRule Linear scan of applicable rules per MExpr RuleMask bitset; skip applied rules
LogicalProps derivation Re-derived per new MExpr Derive once per Group; store Group.logical; memoize
Intern map lookup (HashMap<MExprKey, ExprId>) High insert/lookup rate Pre-size to query complexity; ahash
FuncDepSet::closure() Iterative fixpoint over FD list Early exit on stabilize; small ColSet bitops
ColSet union/intersection Tight inner loops in FD + prop derivation 64-bit SWAR bitset; LLVM autovectorizes u64 arrays
OrderingChoice interning Repeated ordering equality checks Intern by sorted column sequence

Tracing Methodology

quasar-obs wraps optimize() with tracing spans: optimize_query (full wall time); memo_construct (bind → initial memo, normalization fires); search_task.<TaskKind> (per-task: OptimizeGroup/Expr, ApplyRule …); apply_rule.<RuleName> (per-rule); cost_expr (Coster::cost() per expr); distsql_plan (flow_plan()). QUASAR_LOG=quasar_search=trace emits span events. Replay harness re-runs recorded event log deterministically for profiling without a database.

Allocator Analysis

Component Strategy Rationale
Group arena typed_arena::Arena<Group> (bump, no free) Append-only; 0 per-item overhead
MExpr arena typed_arena::Arena<MExpr> Same
ScalarExpr arena typed_arena::Arena<ScalarExpr> Interned; never freed mid-query
MExpr children SmallVec<[GroupId; 3]> ≤3 children common; avoids heap alloc
Intern map pre-sized HashMap<MExprKey, ExprId> Pre-size at Memo::new(capacity)
Per-query scratch bumpalo::Bump for temp vecs Avoids per-rule heap churn

One Memo per query; arenas dropped after plan extraction; no free list.

Lock Analysis

  • optimize() lock-free on hot path: Memo, Optimizer, Coster owned by calling thread.
  • quasar-cat: Arc<RwLock<Catalog>>, held only during bind (before search), released before optimize(); search never acquires catalog locks.
  • quasar-stats: snapshot cloned at bind into LogicalProps.stats; search reads immutable snapshot.
  • quasar-cache: RwLock<PlanCache>, write lock only on insert/invalidation, concurrent readers on lookup; never held during optimize().

Cache Analysis

  • GroupId/ExprId are u32; arrays indexed by these are cache-friendly (no pointer chasing).
  • ColSet as u64 array (u128 for ≤128 cols) fits L1/L2; bitset ops stay in registers.
  • Group.winners HashMap<PhysProps, Winner> typically ≤ 4 entries; linear scan beats hashing for small N — consider SmallVec linear map.
  • RuleMask as u128 bitset (≤128 rules; current ≤ 80) → O(1) already-applied check.

Memory Profiling

  • Tool: dhat / heaptrack. CI reports memo peak bytes per TPC-H query; hard cap 256 MB/query (configurable).
  • Memo bytes = groups.len() * size_of::<Group>() + exprs.len() * size_of::<MExpr>() + intern overhead.
  • Typical: ≤ 50 k MExpr × ~120 B = ~6 MB; well within cap.

Syscall Analysis

  • optimize() performs zero syscalls (pure in-process CPU).
  • Syscalls only at: catalog read (pre-bind), stats load (pre-bind), plan-cache lookup (pre-optimize), FlowSpec serialization (post-optimize).
  • quasar-obs tracing spans use in-process ring buffer; async flush to subscriber (no blocking syscall in optimizer thread).

Tail-Latency Analysis

  • B&B budget: Cost caps worst case: on exhaustion return best plan so far (not error unless none found).
  • reorder_limit: usize (default 8): join groups beyond fall back to greedy enumeration.
  • Normalization rules fire synchronously during memo construction — must be O(1) per application (no iterative fixpoint in one rule).
  • OptimizeGroup short-circuits when group already has a winner for required PhysProps (memoization).

Optimization Levers (Tunable)

Knob Config key Default Effect
Memo budget (MExpr) optimizer.memo_budget 50 000 Hard cap; B&B halts
Cost budget optimizer.cost_budget f64::MAX B&B prunes above
Reorder limit optimizer.reorder_limit 8 Join groups above N → greedy
Intern map capacity optimizer.intern_capacity 1 024 Pre-sizes HashMap
ColSet width compile-time COLSET_WORDS 2 (128 cols) Increase for wide schemas
Stats staleness TTL stats.ttl_secs 3600 Cache invalidation period

Pitfalls

  • Measuring optimize() latency without warm catalog/stats cache misattributes I/O to optimizer; warm separately.
  • criterion wall-clock noise on shared CI runners → false bench-regress; gate on relative % not absolute ns.
  • SmallVec heap spill hard to see in profiles; verify spill rate with a debug counter.
  • Rule application creating memo cycles (rule fires, produces group that re-triggers same rule) must be blocked by RuleMask; missing mask update = infinite loop.
  • FuncDepSet::closure() fixpoint must terminate; guard with iteration-count limit, log if hit (indicates rule producing contradictory FDs).

Scalability Analysis

Dominant Scaling Wall: Join-Order Search

Join reordering is the primary super-exponential bottleneck. Distinct join trees for N tables = N-th Catalan number (C_N ≈ 4^N / (N^1.5 · √π)): C_12 ≈ 208 k, C_16 ≈ 35 M. Cascades rule-based enumeration explores a subset; worst case O(3^N) orderings (all bushy-tree subsets).

N (reorderable joins) Strategy Notes
≤ 8 Full Cascades rule-based exploration Exhaustive; exact optimal
9–16 Cascades + reorder_limit budget cap B&B prunes; near-optimal
17–32 Greedy join enumeration fallback Cheapest-first edge selection
> 32 GEQO-style genetic (deferred, Phase 13) Random plan-space sampling

reorder_limit configurable; exceeding logs warning + reports estimated-only join groups.

Memo Size Scaling

Query type Typical MExpr Memo bytes (est)
Single-table ≤ 50 < 10 KB
4-table join (OLTP) ≤ 500 ~100 KB
8-table join ≤ 5 000 ~1 MB
12-table join (TPC-H complex) ≤ 50 000 ~10 MB
16-table join (hard limit) ≤ 200 000 ~50 MB

Hard limit optimizer.memo_budget = 50 000 MExpr default; configurable to 500 000 for analytical workloads with memory allowance; exceeding → B&B early termination with best-plan-so-far.

ColSet Width Scaling

  • ColSet = fixed-width bitset [u64; COLSET_WORDS]; COLSET_WORDS = 2 → 128 columns.
  • Wide schemas (> 128 cols): raise COLSET_WORDS at compile time (recompile; no runtime penalty for typical schemas).
  • Practical ceiling 512 cols (COLSET_WORDS = 8) before bitset ops dominate FD closure; column pruning (projection pushdown, Phase 4) cuts effective count early.

Horizontal Scaling

Optimizer is stateless per query: all state in per-query Memo + Optimizer, allocated per-call, dropped after extraction. No query-optimizer sharding needed — each gateway independently optimizes its queries.

Dimension Approach Notes
Gateway replication N optimizer instances (one per gateway); no cross-node comms during optimize() Linear throughput scaling
Plan cache quasar-cache per-node (in-process) or shared (Redis/KV) via pluggable backend Shared cuts duplicate optimization
Stats service quasar-stats reads shared store; read-heavy, per-node TTL cache Refresh out-of-band; optimizer never blocks on write
Catalog quasar-cat reads schema; Raft-replicated, read-only during optimize() Schema change → plan-cache invalidation (memo staleness)

Vertical Scaling

Resource Effect Config
CPU Single-threaded optimize() (no search parallelism); higher clock → lower latency N/A
RAM Larger memo_budget+cost_budget → better plan quality at higher latency optimizer.memo_budget, optimizer.cost_budget
L3 cache Larger L3 → lower arena-access latency for large memos Hardware selection

Multi-threaded optimization out of scope; Memo not thread-safe by design (append-only, single writer). Future: per-group parallelism behind RwLock<Group> — not in scope.

DistSQL Flow Fan-Out Scaling

Physical fan-out bounded by distinct leaseholders in a query.

Range count Processors/flow Network streams Notes
1 1–3 0–2 Local plan; no network
2–8 4–16 6–30 Typical distributed OLTP
9–50 17–150 50–500 Analytical fan-out
> 50 Cap at max_processors Aggregation routers reduce fan-in distsql.max_processors

Routers (ByHash, ByRange) reduce fan-in at aggregation nodes. MergeJoiner needs ordered streams from each input → may require Sorter per shard.

Sharding and Partitioning

Component Sharding Notes
Catalog Per-database namespace, each DB independent Multi-tenant: one shard per tenant
Statistics Per-table by TableId in stats store Collection per-range; aggregated offline
Range metadata Partitioned by key range; quasar-cat fetches at bind Leaseholder info cached with TTL
Plan cache Per-node (or per-DB in shared) Key = fingerprint(AST + schema version + stats version)

Replication / Coordination

  • optimize() read-only on all shared state (catalog, stats, range metadata); zero writes during optimization.
  • Plan-cache writes after optimization (store result), local to gateway.
  • Stats updates background/async (not in optimize path); trigger cache invalidation signals.
  • Zero cross-node coordination during optimize().
  • Cache invalidation: push-based via catalog change notifications (schema version bump); each node discards stale entries independently.
  • Stats refresh: pull-based on TTL expiry or explicit ANALYZE.

Network Fanout (DistSQL)

Fan-out bounded by distsql.max_processors (default 64); above, quasar-distsql adds aggregation stages to reduce fan-in. Ordered-stream merges need a Sorter per partition → CPU cost ∝ partition count, accounted in distribution-aware cost model (Phase 11).

Storage Growth

Store Growth driver Eviction
Plan cache (quasar-cache) New unique queries LRU by access; max entries configurable
Stats store ColumnStatistic per table Overwritten per ANALYZE; bounded
Range metadata cache Range splits/merges TTL eviction + background refresh
Optimizer event log (quasar-obs) Per-query emission Fixed-size ring buffer; flush to external sink

Metadata Scaling

Range/leaseholder metadata ∝ cluster size. quasar-cat caches range metadata with TTL (default 30 s); 1 000-node / 100 k-range cluster ≈ ~10 MB cached metadata per gateway. Above 1 M ranges: lazy per-table range fetch (only tables referenced in query) via quasar-cat per-table range lookup API.

Pitfalls

  • reorder_limit too high (e.g. 32) with no cost budget → optimize() hangs on star-schema; always pair reorder_limit with cost_budget.
  • Shared plan cache across gateways → distributed cache-consistency problem; prefer per-node unless hit rate demonstrably low.
  • Stats TTL too short → excessive refreshes under high load → catalog I/O bottleneck; tune TTL to ANALYZE cadence.
  • COLSET_WORDS change requires full recompile of all crates; document as breaking workspace-level change.
  • max_processors must count scan + join/aggregation processors; miscount → under-cap + excessive network fan-in.

Mental Models & First Principles

Expert compressions that predict system behavior. Each: the insight, what it predicts, the bug class written without it, and the §/phase in Quasar where it is applied.

MM1 — A Memo Is a Finite Representation of an Infinite Plan Space The insight: a memo group represents every logically equivalent expression simultaneously; the memo encodes exponentially many alternative plans in polynomial space via structural sharing. What it predicts: join-order enumeration of N tables fits in O(N²) groups and O(N³) MExprs rather than requiring N! separate trees. Bug class without it: building one plan tree per candidate (no sharing) → OOM on 6-table queries, no B&B possible. Resolving §: §15 (Phase 3) memo Group/MExpr arena + interning design; §22 (Phase 10) Cascades task-stack search over memo.

MM2 — Cost Monotonicity Is the Soul of Branch-and-Bound The insight: if adding any operator to a partial plan never decreases total cost (I4), then a partial plan already exceeding the current best complete plan can be safely abandoned — its completion cannot improve. What it predicts: B&B prunes entire subtrees, not just leaves; pruning ratio grows with the number of tables. Bug class without it: a cost function that subtracts from children (e.g., modelling a "cached result discount") invalidates B&B → optimizer returns suboptimal plans while believing they are optimal. Resolving §: §7 I4 (cost monotonicity invariant); §22 (Phase 10 quasar-search B&B pruning logic).

MM3 — Cardinality Error Compounds Multiplicatively Across Joins The insight: if each join's cardinality is estimated with relative error ε, an n-join query accumulates error ~εⁿ; a 3× per-join error becomes 243× on a 5-join query (Leis et al. 2015 JOB result). What it predicts: plans break on multi-join queries long before they break on single-table queries. Bug class without it: calibrating the estimator only on single-table selectivity benchmarks → catastrophically wrong join-order choices in prod. Resolving §: §17 (Phase 5) multi-column stats, q-error acceptance criterion §4.7, Leis et al. 2015 citation.

MM4 — Physical Properties Are Required → Propagated Downward, Provided → Checked Upward The insight: a required ordering/distribution flows top-down from the root; each operator either provides it natively or an enforcer (Sort/Distribute) must be inserted to bridge the gap. Provided properties flow bottom-up from leaves. What it predicts: if a sort is needed, exactly one Sort enforcer appears at the correct place — never "maybe sort somewhere." Bug class without it: checking properties after plan selection rather than during → either wrong results (MergeJoin on unsorted input) or redundant sorts. Resolving §: §21 (Phase 9 PhysProps + enforcers); §22 (Phase 10 EnforceProps task); §7 I5, I12.

MM5 — Normalization, Exploration, and Implementation Must Be Strictly Separated The insight: normalization (always-on, confluent, cost-reducing) is safe to fire unconditionally; exploration (logical→logical, cost-driven) expands the space; implementation (logical→physical, cost-driven) concretizes it. Mixing them corrupts confluence guarantees. What it predicts: a normalize rule that is not confluent causes the plan to depend on insertion order → non-deterministic plans. Bug class without it: placing an exploration rule in the Normalize pass → normalize fires it without cost context → possible plan blowup or infinite loop. Resolving §: §6 Principle 3; §16 (Phase 4) optgen rule-kind tagging; §7 I3.

MM6 — Network Cost Dominates in Geo-Distributed Plans The insight: for a cross-region query, the network cost of transferring rows between regions (milliseconds per round-trip, bandwidth ceiling) dwarfs CPU sort cost or IO seek cost by 1–2 orders of magnitude. What it predicts: locality-optimized plans (LookupJoin over local index vs. HashJoin shuffling rows globally) win on latency even when cardinality naively favors hash. Bug class without it: treating net cost as zero → optimizer always chooses hash joins → every query fans out globally → 10–100× latency. Resolving §: §23 (Phase 11) net-cost model + LOS algorithm; §8 Distribution model; §9 ADR-A08 three-component Cost struct.

MM7 — Structural Deduplication Is the Memo's Correctness Invariant The insight: two expressions with identical (op, children, scalar) must map to exactly one ExprId (I1); otherwise the same expression is explored and costed twice, winner tables diverge, and plan extraction is ambiguous. What it predicts: the intern map is a precondition for correctness of the entire optimizer, not a performance optimization. Bug class without it: duplicate ExprIds for the same expression → B&B computes inconsistent lower bounds → prunes valid plans. Resolving §: §15 (Phase 3) Memo::intern() + MExprKey hash; §7 I1 detail.

MM8 — Generic Plans Are Safe; Custom Plans Are Optimal; Neither Is Both The insight: a generic plan (no specific placeholder values) must be correct for all type-compatible bindings, so it uses conservative cardinality; a custom plan (specific values) is optimal for that binding but wrong for others. The switch point is when value-specific selectivity would change the plan by more than 10× (ADR-A19). What it predicts: for queries with index scans on selective keys, custom plans are materially faster; for queries where selectivity is uniform regardless of value, generic plans are identical quality. Bug class without it: always using generic plans → misses index utilization on selective OLTP lookups by 10–100×. Resolving §: §25 (Phase 13) quasar-cache generic/custom plan logic; §9 ADR-A19; §7 I8.

MM9 — Determinism Is a Property of the Algorithm, Not the Data The insight: an optimizer that gives the same answer for the same inputs (sql, catalog, stats, seed) makes golden testing, replay, and cost-regression attribution possible; any source of non-determinism (HashMap iteration, thread-local RNG, pointer identity as tie-break) destroys that property. What it predicts: if any non-deterministic path exists in quasar-search or quasar-distsql, the replay harness will eventually catch a divergence. Bug class without it: std::collections::HashMap iteration in rule dispatch → plans differ across runs → CI golden files flake, cost regressions cannot be attributed to a specific commit. Resolving §: §6 Principle 4; §7 I10; §25 (Phase 13) replay harness; §9 ADR-A17.

MM10 — Cascade of Failures: Stale Stats → Wrong Cardinality → Wrong Join Order → Catastrophic Plan The insight: the entire cost-based path is only as good as its statistics input; stale stats do not fail loudly — they silently produce wrong cardinality estimates that cascade into wrong join orders that produce plans 100–1000× slower than optimal. What it predicts: plan quality regressions after large data loads are almost always a stats-freshness problem, not an optimizer bug. Bug class without it: logging only OptError failures while ignoring is_estimated=true → silent plan quality degradation after ANALYZE is skipped. Resolving §: §5 Leis et al. 2015; §17 (Phase 5) is_estimated flag propagation; §4.7 q-error targets; §7 Principle 7 Statistics Honesty.

Comparative Architecture Table

This optimizer vs. five real production query optimizers across load-bearing architectural dimensions.

Dimension Quasar (this blueprint) PostgreSQL Planner Apache Calcite (VolcanoPlanner) CockroachDB Optimizer Trino / Presto Greenplum GPORCA
Search strategy Top-down Cascades/memo; branch-and-bound Bottom-up DP (Selinger 1979); geqo for large N Top-down Cascades-style VolcanoPlanner Top-down Cascades; optgen DSL Heuristic RBO + limited CBO; no full Cascades Top-down Cascades (Orca); parallel task scheduler
Memo structure Group/MExpr typed arenas; u32 handles; structural intern Path list per relation (no memo); equivalence classes for ordering RelSet/RelSubset (Java objects); RelNode DAG with trait sets Group/MExpr (Go map); same Cascades lineage as Quasar No memo; single plan tree; rule-based rewrites Memo with DXL serialization; separate optimizer process
Cost model 3-component {cpu, io, net}; distribution-aware net multiplier GUC-tunable {seq_page_cost, random_page_cost, cpu_tuple_cost}; no net cost Pluggable RelMetadataQuery; convention-based cost via traits Similar to Quasar; {cpu, io, net, total}; locality-aware net Row-count based; memory estimates; no distribution cost 3-component; distribution-aware; broadcast vs. hash modeled
Statistics/cardinality HLL NDV + equi-depth histograms + multi-column ColSet stats MCV + equi-depth histograms; extended stats (pg_statistic_ext) RelMdRowCount + column metadata; pluggable metadata handlers Same histogram format as Quasar; HLL NDV Column-level statistics from connectors; no cross-column Column-level + multi-column; sampling-based
Rule engine optgen DSL → build-time Rust codegen; 3 rule kinds (Normalize/Explore/Implement) Hard-coded C path rewrites; equivalence-based join reordering Java rule classes (RelRule/ConverterRule); runtime registration optgen DSL (Go codegen); same separation as Quasar Java rule-based (OptimizerRule interface); runtime registration C++ rule classes (CXform hierarchy)
Distributed / exchange operators Distribute enforcer; ByHash/ByRange/Mirror/PassThrough routers; FlowSpec Not distributed natively; partitioning via foreign-data wrappers Spark/Flink conventions via CalciteConvention system DistSQL processors/streams; leaseholder-aware placement Fragment/stage split; exchange operators with partitioning Broadcast/hash/singleton motion operators; DXL plan format
Join enumeration Cascades transformation rules (JoinCommutativity + JoinAssociate); reorder_limit B&B cap GEQO above join_collapse_limit (default 8); DP below DP + rule-based; Calcite LoptOptimizeJoinRule Same Cascades rules; DPhyp noted as rejected alternative Left-deep heuristic; limited bushy tree exploration Cascades join enumeration; handles outer/semi/anti joins
Deployment model Library crate; stateless per-query optimize(); no daemon Embedded in backend process; planner not separable Library jar; embedded in Hive/Flink/Drill via Calcite API Embedded in CockroachDB SQL gateway Coordinator process; planner in coordinator node Standalone optimizer process communicating via DXL/protobuf

PostgreSQL planner. The Selinger bottom-up DP approach was deliberately retained because PostgreSQL's workload is OLTP-dominant: ≤8-table queries, all inner equi-joins, no multi-region distribution. Bottom-up DP is faster to implement, produces optimal join orders within its regime, and has 30+ years of production validation. Its niche is single-node, well-understood schemas where adding a Cascades engine would triple optimizer complexity for no plan-quality gain.

Apache Calcite. Calcite's key differentiating choice is the open RelTrait/RelTraitSet convention system — any rule can introduce a new "physical convention" (e.g., SparkConvention, JdbcConvention) without modifying the planner. This wins in federated query systems (Hive, Flink, Drill, Avatica) where the same logical plan must be executed across heterogeneous engines; the convention-based matching bridges them. The cost is verbose Java boilerplate and a metadata-handler chain that is fragile across optimizer versions.

CockroachDB optimizer. Quasar's direct ancestor; the primary reference implementation (Taft et al. 2020). The key difference is language (Go vs. Rust) and memory management: Go's GC simplifies rule authoring at the cost of latency tail spikes on complex queries. The optgen DSL and memo structure are nearly identical. CockroachDB wins when the optimizer must be co-located with a Go-based SQL layer where an FFI boundary to Rust is operationally costly.

Trino / Presto. Trino's architectural differentiator is the connector abstraction: it plans and executes queries over arbitrary external data sources (Hive, Iceberg, Delta, Kafka) by treating each as a ConnectorMetadata + ConnectorSplit source. The optimizer is intentionally simpler (rule-based rewrites, no full Cascades memo) because plan quality is secondary to connector I/O latency and the federated data diversity makes calibrated cost models impractical. Trino wins in interactive analytics over data lakes where connector flexibility matters more than join-order optimality.

Greenplum GPORCA. GPORCA's defining choice is the standalone optimizer process communicating via DXL (XML-like IR) over an IPC boundary. This wins in a massively parallel processing (MPP) database where the optimizer must be independently upgradable, testable, and deployable without restarting the database cluster. The parallel Cascades task scheduler in GPORCA also handles 200+ table analytic queries that would exhaust a single-threaded budget — at the cost of complex cross-thread group locking.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived step-by-step from §4 System Constraints. Formula shown; no asserted-only values.

1. Memo size for a 12-table join query

From §4.4: reorderable join count ≤ 32; §4.3 MExpr count / query ≤ 131,072.

  • Groups: each of 12 tables = 1 Scan group; each 2-table join subset = C(12,2) = 66 groups; 3-table = C(12,3) = 220; exploraton adds ~3× alternatives per join = ~858 logical MExprs for join groups alone.
  • MExpr count estimate: N-table query → ~3^N groups explored (worst-case Cascades bushy) = 3^12 = 531,441 but B&B prunes ~80%; practical count ≈ 531,441 × 0.20 ≈ 106,000 MExprs — near the 131,072 cap, explaining why the default reorder_limit = 12 was chosen.
  • RAM per MExpr: MExpr struct = Operator(4B) + SmallVec<[GroupId;3]>(~28B) + Option<ScalarId>(4B) + RuleMask(16B) = ~52 B; round to 64 B with alignment.
  • Memo RAM for 12-table join: 106,000 × 64 B = 6.8 MB — well within the 64 MB memo_byte_budget.

2. Planning latency budget per task

From §4.1: 10-table join p95 target = 30 ms; §4.9 task_budget default = 100,000.

  • Available time per task: 30 ms / 100,000 tasks = 0.30 µs/task.
  • OptimizeGroup is the hottest task; it checks winners (O(1) HashMap lookup) + pushes child tasks. At 0.30 µs, one Group winner lookup + 3 child task pushes must complete in 300 ns — achievable on modern hardware (L1 cache hit ~1 ns, L2 ~5 ns, task Vec push ~10 ns).

3. Histogram storage for a 100-table catalog

From §4.3 plan cache and §5 references: equi-depth histogram default = 100 buckets/column; §4.4 column count/query ≤ ~1024, but a typical 100-table schema has ~10 columns/table.

  • Columns: 100 tables × 10 cols/table = 1,000 columns.
  • Bucket size: Bucket { upper_bound: Datum(~16B), num_eq: f64(8B), num_range: f64(8B), distinct_range: f64(8B) } = 40 B/bucket.
  • Per-column histogram: 100 buckets × 40 B = 4,000 B.
  • Total stats storage: 1,000 cols × 4,000 B = 4 MB — fits in L3 cache; explains why quasar-stats can load stats in < 20 ms (§4.5).

4. Plan cache memory footprint

From §4.3: plan cache default 256 MB; a typical FlowSpec for an 8-table TPC-H query has ~20 processors × ProcessorSpec(~200 B) + 30 streams × Stream(~40 B) = ~5,200 B ≈ 6 KB/plan.

  • Entries at 256 MB: 256 × 1024 × 1024 / 6,144 = 43,690 cached plans — a large prepared-statement workload (10,000 distinct queries × multiple schema versions) fits comfortably.

5. Rule-application throughput bound

From §4.2: simple 1–2 table queries at > 5,000 optimize()/sec/core. A simple query fires ~20 rules.

  • Rule applications/sec: 5,000 × 20 = 100,000 rule applications/sec.
  • Time per rule application: 1 / 100,000 = 10 µs/application — this is the latency budget for ApplyRule including pattern-match + Memo::intern + LogicalProps derivation. Profiling target.

6. Network cost dominance crossover

From §4.1: 10-table join p50 = 10 ms; §9 ADR-A08: total = cpu + io + w_net × net; typical w_net calibrated so 1 MB cross-region = 10 ms latency cost at 100 Mbps inter-region bandwidth.

  • Cross-region row transfer breakeven: if a HashJoin ships 10,000 rows × 100 B/row = 1 MB across regions = 10 ms net cost; a LookupJoin with 10,000 lookups × 0.5 ms/lookup = 5,000 ms CPU cost is worse. Breakeven at ~200 rows × 100 B = 20 KB → above 20 KB shipped, hash shuffle beats per-row lookup; below 20 KB, LookupJoin wins.
  • This explains why LOS prefers LookupJoin when the input cardinality is low (< ~200 filtered rows), which is exactly the OLTP primary-key lookup pattern.

7. ColSet bitset operations per FD closure step

From §11.1: ColSet = 2 × u64 (128-bit); FD closure iterates deps to fixed-point.

  • Bitwise OR of two ColSets: 2 × 64-bit OR = 2 instructions on any modern CPU; negligible.
  • Fixed-point convergence: typical schema has ≤ 10 FD rules; closure converges in ≤ 3 iterations = 3 × 10 × 2 instrs = 60 instructions per FD closure call — under 20 ns at 3 GHz.
  • This confirms §9 ADR-A05 (arena + u32 handles are cache-friendly; ColSet ops stay in registers).

8. Span enumeration ceiling for IN-list predicate

From §4.4: IN-list size before span collapse ≤ 10,000 values; §4.4 index key column count ≤ 16/index.

  • Spans from WHERE a IN (v1…v10000) on a 2-column composite index with range on col2: 10,000 point spans × open range on col2 = 10,000 spans × 2 endpoints = 20,000 key comparisons for span sort/dedup.
  • At 50 ns/comparison: 20,000 × 50 ns = 1 ms just for span normalization — this explains the collapse threshold and the §4.4 cap at 10,000 IN-list values.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each question requires synthesis of ≥2 sections. The resolving § is named; no question is answerable from a single line.

Q1. A 10-table TPC-H query regresses from 8 ms to 240 ms optimize() latency after a stats refresh. No code changed. Walk through how stale-to-fresh stats could cause this regression and how you would diagnose it. (Requires §17 stats/cardinality + §22 Cascades search + §4.7 q-error targets + §7 I4 cost monotonicity)

Q2. Explain why HashMap iteration order in quasar-search is a correctness defect, not merely a performance concern. What specific invariant does it violate, and how does the replay harness catch it? (Requires §6 Principle 4 + §7 I10 + §25 Phase 13 replay + §9 ADR-A17)

Q3. A new operator WindowAgg must be implemented. List every file that must change, the invariant checks that must be extended, and the conformance fixtures that must be added. (Requires §10 repo layout + §7 I2 group props uniformity + §8.2 operator table + §16 Phase 4 optgen)

Q4. Why does B&B require I4 (cost monotonicity) as a mathematical precondition, not just a performance guideline? Give a concrete example of a cost function that violates it and show how the resulting plan could be sub-optimal. (Requires §7 I4 + §7 I7 + §22 Phase 10 B&B search + §19 Phase 7 cost model)

Q5. A query SELECT * FROM t WHERE a IN (1,2,…,15000) on an index idx(a ASC) produces a full table scan instead of an index scan. Trace exactly where this happens in the optimizer pipeline and justify why it is correct behavior. (Requires §4.4 IN-list cap + §18 Phase 6 constraint solver + §6 Principle 1 cost-based + §7 I5)

Q6. The LOS algorithm prefers a LookupJoin over a HashJoin for a cross-region query. A teammate argues the HashJoin is cheaper by CPU cost. Explain the precise condition under which LOS is correct and your teammate is wrong, with numbers from the capacity model. (Requires §23 Phase 11 LOS + §6 Principle 8 distribution + §8.7 distribution model + Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model item 6 "Network cost dominance crossover")

Q7. Describe the complete lifecycle of a normalization rule from .opt source file to execution inside Memo::intern. At which step could a confluence violation silently cause non-deterministic plans, and how is it caught? (Requires §16 Phase 4 optgen + §7 I3 + §6 Principle 3 + §22 Phase 10)

Q8. A prepared statement SELECT * FROM orders WHERE customer_id = $1 is cached as a generic plan. Under what exact conditions does quasar-cache switch to a custom plan, and what guarantee does the generic plan provide for all bindings? (Requires §25 Phase 13 plan cache + §9 ADR-A19 + §7 I8 + §11.11 OptError)

Q9. Explain how functional dependencies enable OrderingChoice simplification and give a concrete example showing a 3-column ordering that reduces to 1 column after FD application. What invariant is maintained, and what happens if it is violated? (Requires §14 Phase 2 FD derivation + §7 I6 + §6 Principle 5 + §21 Phase 9 interesting orderings)

Q10. A CockroachDB engineer reports that Quasar produces different FlowSpecs for the same query across two otherwise identical gateway nodes. You enable QUASAR_LOG=trace. What are the three most likely root causes, in order of probability, and how do you rule each out? (Requires §7 I10 + §6 Principle 4 + §25 Phase 13 replay + §9 ADR-A17 + §9 ADR-A18)

Q11. Explain why MemoStale is returned rather than silently re-using a cached plan when schema_version changes, and describe the exact scenario where using the stale plan would produce wrong results rather than merely sub-optimal results. (Requires §25 Phase 13 cache invalidation + §9 ADR-A19 + §7 I8 + §8 domain model §8.6 constraint)

Q12. A new multi-column statistic must be added for (city, zip_code) after finding that existing plans produce a catastrophic cross-join due to a correlated predicate. Trace every component from quasar-stats through quasar-search that would change, and justify why the independence assumption fails for this column pair. (Requires §17 Phase 5 stats + §6 Principle 7 + §7 I2 LogicalProps + §5.2 Leis et al. 2015)

Q13. Why must EnforceProps be a separate task type rather than being handled inside OptimizeExpr? What would break if enforcers were inserted greedily at physical-plan selection time rather than during search? (Requires §22 Phase 10 task state machine + §8.8 task state machine + §21 Phase 9 + §7 I5 + §7 I12)

Q14. Quasar's B&B budget is exhausted on a 20-table star-schema query. What plan is returned, and what guarantee (if any) does that plan carry? Under which knob configuration could you trade higher memory for better plan quality, and what is the cost of that trade? (Requires §6 Principle 6 + §4.9 budget knobs + §7 I7 + §22 Phase 10 budget exhaustion + §4.1 latency targets)

Q15. The quasar-distsql placement algorithm assigns P3: TableReader{order_item lookup} to node-2 rather than to the gateway. Explain exactly why, what catalog query is made to determine this, and what would happen to the FlowSpec if leaseholder metadata were unavailable. (Requires §24 Phase 12 DistSQL placement + §8.7 distribution model + §3.2 quasar-cat description + §9 ADR-A16)

Q16. A rule PushLimitIntoScan is added as a Normalize rule but accidentally increases expression size under some conditions. Trace the exact failure mode through the invariant system, which fuzzer target catches it, and what metric appears in quasar-obs when it fires. (Requires §6 Principle 3 + §7 I3 + §9 ADR-A04 + §25 Phase 13 fuzzing + §7 I4)

Q17. Explain why the Cascades framework naturally handles the Distribution physical property while the original Volcano framework (Graefe & McKenna 1993) does not, and why this matters specifically for Quasar's DistSQL planning. (Requires §5.2 Volcano paper + §5.2 Cascades paper + §6 Principle 8 + §23 Phase 11 + §8.7)

Q18. A stats collection daemon updates histograms for orders.amount while a long-running analytics query is mid-optimization. Explain what happens to the in-flight optimization, what happens to the next query, and which invariant protects the in-flight query from seeing inconsistent data. (Requires §4.6 determinism + §7 I10 + §3.3 concurrency boundary + §25 Phase 13 cache invalidation)

Q19. You must add a ZigzagJoin physical operator that intersects two index scans. List the exact set of quasar-rules .opt entries needed, the PhysProps it provides, and the conformance directives required to verify its correctness. (Requires §8.2 operator table + §21 Phase 9 + §10 repo layout + §9 ADR-A04 + §7 I5)

Q20. How does the optgen build-time hash check prevent a "split-brain" between .opt source and committed generated Rust code? Give the exact sequence of CI events that catches a developer who edits normalize.opt but forgets to regenerate. (Requires §16 Phase 4 + §9 ADR-A04 + Build & Release Engineering → Build Graph + Failure Modes & Recovery Class H)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alternative 1 — Heuristic / Rule-Only Planner (no cost model)

SQL → parse → bind → fixed rule-pass pipeline
 Rule 1: predicate pushdown (always)
 Rule 2: index selection (first applicable)
 Rule 3: join ordering (left-deep, left-to-right)
 Rule 4: aggregation pushdown (if GROUP BY key is indexed)
 → single physical plan (no alternatives explored)

Regime where it wins: embedded systems with ≤ 4 tables, simple OLTP SELECT/INSERT, predictable schema, latency budget < 0.1 ms (RBO fires in microseconds; CBO adds 1–10 ms). Real system: early MySQL query optimizer (pre-5.6); SQLite; many microcontroller-embedded SQL engines. Why this blueprint rejected it: Quasar targets distributed, multi-table analytical queries where join order dominates performance by orders of magnitude — a heuristic planner would pick the wrong join order on >50% of TPC-H queries. The entire value proposition (CockroachDB lineage) is cost-based optimization for geo-distributed workloads.

Alternative 2 — Bottom-Up Dynamic Programming (System R / Selinger style)

            Enumerate all (left, right) join subsets
            bottom-up: smallest subsets first
           ┌──────────────┐
table t1 ──►  DP[{t1}]    │
table t2 ──►  DP[{t2}]    │  join DP[{t1}]+DP[{t2}] → DP[{t1,t2}]
table t3 ──►  DP[{t3}]    │  join DP[{t1,t2}]+DP[{t3}] → DP[{t1,t2,t3}]
           └──────────────┘
            Optimal substructure: DP[S] = min over all splits of S

Regime where it wins: OLTP workloads with ≤ 12 inner equi-joins, no complex physical properties beyond ordering, single-node execution (no distribution property to track). Real system: PostgreSQL planner (Selinger DP + geqo); IBM DB2's join enumerator. Why this blueprint rejected it: bottom-up DP is goal-blind (it computes optimal plans for all subsets, even those the root query never requests); it does not naturally support goal-directed optimization for specific physical properties (Distribution); and it cannot interleave join reordering with predicate pushdown and index-scan generation in a single pass. ADR-A06 captures this.

Alternative 3 — Query Compilation (no Volcano/Cascades interpreter)

SQL → parse → bind → compile to native code
 ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ LLVM IR generation per query       │
 │ • inner loops compiled, not inter- │
 │   preted                           │
 │ • no Volcano iterator overhead     │
 │ • vectorized tight loops           │
 └────────────────────────────────────┘
 → compiled binary (run once per query)

Regime where it wins: ultra-high-throughput OLAP on a fixed schema with repeated query templates where JIT compilation amortizes over millions of executions per query shape; latency budget allows 50–200 ms compile overhead. Real system: HyPer (Neumann 2011 VLDB, "Efficiently Compiling Efficient Query Plans"); Umbra; NoisePage. Why this blueprint rejected it: Quasar is an optimizer, not an execution engine (P12 — no execution coupling); the FlowSpec is handed to project 318 for execution. Query compilation is an execution-engine technique. Additionally, compilation latency (50–200 ms per query) violates the §4.1 optimize() latency targets (p99 < 10 ms for OLTP queries).

Alternative 4 — Learned / ML-Based Cost Model (no hand-calibrated coefficients)

Query features → neural network → predicted cost
 ┌────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ Features: operator type, cardinality,  │
 │   index selectivity, join depth,       │
 │   data distribution embeddings         │
 │                                        │
 │ Model: gradient-boosted trees or       │
 │   small MLP trained on EXPLAIN ANALYZE │
 │   feedback                             │
 └────────────────────────────────────────┘

Regime where it wins: high-volume SaaS workloads with stable query templates where EXPLAIN ANALYZE feedback accumulates quickly, correlation between features and actual cost is learnable, and the 2–5× q-error reduction (Marcus et al. 2021, Bao system) is worth the ML infrastructure dependency. Real system: Bao (Marcus et al. 2021 SIGMOD); Microsoft's Bao-influenced hints in Azure SQL; Neo (Marcus et al. 2019). Why this blueprint rejected it: ML models require inference runtimes (ONNX, PyTorch), training pipelines, and labeled data — all out of scope for a foundation optimizer library. The calibrated-coefficient model (ADR-A12, costs.toml) is interpretable, tunable by operators, and requires no feedback loop. A learned model defers gracefully to Phase 13+ as an extension via the Coster trait.

Deferred Features Register

All features marked PhaseDeferred(...) in the blueprint, with their anchor, building phase, and the condition that would un-defer them.

Feature Anchor (PhaseDeferred token) Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
GPU/SIMD vectorized cost paths PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25") Phase 13 / §25 Project 318 (Adaptive Query Execution) exposes a vectorized execution model with measurable per-operator SIMD speedups; quasar-cost adds a simd_capable: bool field to BaseCoster
Online stats auto-collection daemon PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25") Phase 13 / §25 A background stats-collection process is built (separate project); quasar-stats gains a subscribe_stats_refresh() API to receive push notifications rather than polling
Cross-region 2PC cost modelling PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §23") Phase 11 / §23 Project 196 (Distributed Transaction Coordinator) exposes TrueTime commit-latency estimates; quasar-cost gains a two_pc_latency_ms(RegionId, RegionId) -> f64 Coster extension
Learned cardinality (ML) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25") Phase 13 / §25 An inference runtime (e.g. ONNX) is integrated into the portfolio; quasar-stats gains a LearnedEstimator trait implementing ColumnStatistic backed by a trained model; existing calibrated estimator remains the fallback
Full SQL grammar (all clauses) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §13") Phase 1 / §13 Projects 259/313 deliver a full SQL parser; quasar-sql is replaced or extended with the full grammar; WINDOW, LATERAL, TABLESAMPLE, MERGE, RETURNING all land
WINDOW function full support PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25") Phase 13 / §25 Execution engine (project 318) implements window function operators; quasar-rules gains WindowAgg exploration and implementation rules; Window(WindowOp) RelExpr variant un-stubbed
GEQO-style genetic join enumeration PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §25") Phase 13 / §25 Query workload contains routinely > 32 reorderable joins (OLAP star-schema superset); current greedy fallback produces measurably worse plans; genetic sampling added as a third enumeration path above reorder_limit

Glossary

Implementation-relevant definitions, as used across §12–§25. Terms in bold elsewhere in the document resolve here.

  • Bound IR — the typed relational+scalar tree (RelExpr/ScalarExpr) produced by quasar-bind from raw SQL AST; the input to the memo. Names resolved to ColumnId, types checked.
  • Branch-and-bound (B&B) — the search pruning technique: pass a cost upper bound down; abandon any expression whose lower_bound cost ≥ the current best. Guarantees optimality within budget (I7) and bounds tail latency.
  • Cardinality — estimated row count of a relational expression. Tracked as a bound (Cardinality{min,max}) in logical properties and as an estimate (Statistics.row_count) from stats.
  • Cascades — the Graefe optimizer framework Quasar implements: a memo of equivalent expressions explored top-down by cost-based transformation/implementation rules with branch-and-bound. Successor to Volcano.
  • Coster — the trait (quasar-cost) that assigns a Cost to a physical expression given stats + provided properties; also yields the B&B lower bound.
  • Cost — {cpu, io, net, total}; total is the sole comparison key. net dominates distributed plans.
  • Custom plan — a plan re-optimized with concrete placeholder values bound (vs a generic plan). Chosen when value-specific selectivity would change the plan materially (§25).
  • Distribution (property) — a physical property describing which region(s) a relation's rows reside in: Any | Single(region) | Set(regions). Required at the root (gateway), provided by scans via leaseholder locality.
  • Distribute (enforcer) — a physical operator inserted to change a relation's distribution (repartition/shuffle) when provided ≠ required. Cost ∝ bytes × region distance.
  • DistSQL — the distributed physical execution layer; Quasar's output is a FlowSpec for it (processors + streams + routers placed on nodes).
  • Enforcer — a physical operator (Sort, Distribute) inserted by the search to satisfy a required physical property the input does not already provide.
  • Equi-depth histogram — per-column stat where each bucket holds ~equal row count; used for range/equality selectivity. Bucket = {upper_bound, num_eq, num_range, distinct_range}.
  • Exploration rule — a cost-based logical→logical transformation (join commutativity/associativity, GenerateIndexScan) fired during search; expands the memo with equivalent alternatives. Tagged [Explore].
  • Functional dependency (FD) — a relationship X → Y meaning X-values determine Y-values. Tracked in FuncDepSet (keys, equivalencies, constants, strict deps); drives ordering simplification and key derivation.
  • FD closure — the full set of columns determined by a column set under a FuncDepSet; used to simplify orderings (I6) and validate keys (I11).
  • FlowSpec — Quasar's stable wire output: the distributed physical flow (processors, streams, gateway) handed to the execution engine (project 318).
  • Generic plan — a plan built treating placeholders as unknown-but-typed (default cardinality assumptions), reusable across bindings (§25).
  • Group — a memo node representing a set of logically equivalent expressions; owns one LogicalProps shared by all members (I2), plus per-required-props winners.
  • GroupId / ExprId — u32 arena indices into a Memo; valid only within that memo; never serialized raw or compared across memos.
  • HyperLogLog (HLL) — probabilistic sketch used offline to estimate NDV (distinct count) per column with bounded memory; result stored in ColumnStatistic.distinct_count.
  • Implementation rule — a logical→physical transformation (InnerJoin→HashJoin/MergeJoin/LookupJoin, GroupBy→HashAgg/StreamAgg) fired during search. Tagged [Implement].
  • Index constraint — a Constraint (spans over an index key prefix) derived from a filter by the constraint solver, plus a remaining (residual) filter.
  • Interesting ordering — an ordering that a downstream operator (merge join, stream aggregation, ORDER BY) could exploit; collected and requested from children to avoid sorts.
  • Leaseholder — the replica of a range that serves reads/writes; its node's locality determines a scan's provided distribution. From quasar-cat placement metadata.
  • Locality-optimized search (LOS) — for a LIMIT over a globally-distributed table, scan the local region first and fan out only if under-filled; avoids cross-region traffic (§23).
  • Logical properties — derived facts true of every member of a group: out_cols, not_null, keys, FDs, cardinality bounds, outer_cols, stats. Computed once (I2).
  • Lookup join — an index nested-loop join: for each input row, look up matching rows via an index. Distributed-favorable (one round trip per row vs shipping a whole table).
  • Memo — the central data structure: interned arenas of groups and expressions encoding the entire equivalent-plan space compactly with structural sharing.
  • Memo staleness — a cached memo/plan whose schema or stats version no longer matches the catalog; detected by fingerprint mismatch (MemoStale), triggering invalidation (§25, Compatibility & Versioning).
  • MExpr — a memo expression (member): an operator + child GroupIds + optional scalar payload + rules_applied mask.
  • NDV (number of distinct values) — distinct count of a column; the denominator for equality selectivity (≈ 1/NDV) and group-by output cardinality.
  • Normalization rule — an always-on, confluent, cost-reducing canonicalization fired in the construct factory during memo building (predicate pushdown, constant folding, projection pruning). Tagged [Normalize].
  • optgen — Quasar's rule DSL compiler (quasar-optgen): parses .opt files and generates Rust match/replace dispatch code at build time. Deterministic codegen.
  • Optimization goal — a (group, required PhysProps) pair; the unit the search optimizes and memoizes a Winner for.
  • Ordering choice — OrderingChoice: an FD-simplified required/provided ordering (constant and equivalent columns dropped) so it hashes canonically as a winners key.
  • Outer columns — columns referenced by a subquery but bound in an enclosing scope; the correlation marker tracked in logical properties.
  • Physical properties — PhysProps: ordering + distribution + limit hint. Required props pushed top-down; provided props derived bottom-up; gaps filled by enforcers.
  • Placeholder — a query parameter ($1) bound at execution; typed at bind time, valued at plan/execute time. Drives generic-vs-custom plan choice.
  • Processor — a DistSQL flow node executing one physical operation on one node (TableReader, HashJoiner, Aggregator, Sorter, …).
  • Provided vs required properties — provided = what an expression actually delivers; required = what its consumer demands. Search reconciles them, inserting enforcers when provided ⊉ required.
  • q-error — the multiplicative error of a cardinality estimate (max(est/actual, actual/est)); the standard estimator-quality metric. Target: within an order of magnitude (§4).
  • Range — a contiguous key span of a table, the unit of distribution/replication; each range has a leaseholder. Scans map to ranges → processors.
  • Router — directs a processor's output streams: PassThrough, ByHash(cols) (repartition), Mirror (broadcast), ByRange(spans).
  • Selectivity — the fraction of rows a predicate passes ([0,1]); the multiplier applied to input cardinality to estimate output rows.
  • Span — a half-open/closed key interval over an index key prefix ({start, start_boundary, end, end_boundary}); the constraint solver's output unit.
  • Tight constraint — a Constraint that fully captures its source predicate (remaining filter empty); a loose constraint leaves a residual filter to evaluate.
  • Volcano — Graefe's earlier optimizer-generator framework (bottom-up); the predecessor to Cascades. Quasar uses Cascades (top-down) but shares the rule/property vocabulary.
  • Winner — the lowest-cost expression for a (group, required props) goal, with its cost and provided props; the unit of the extracted plan tree.
  • Zone config — per-table/index region affinity + replication settings in quasar-cat; the input that makes plans locality-aware (§23).

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

One row per phase, matching §12–§25. Durations are engineering estimates for a focused implementer; they sum to the §1 timeline (Phase 0 ≈ 1–2 weeks; full build ≈ months). Key Risks distill each phase's Pitfalls/Failure Analysis; Exit Criteria distill each phase's Acceptance Criteria.

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
0 — Walking Skeleton Workspace + 16 crate stubs; SELECT a FROM t WHERE a>1 → bind → 1-member memo → trivial cost → single TableReader FlowSpec → EXPLAIN 1–2 wk over-scaffolding; premature abstractions make build/test/run green; quasar opt/explain print a plan end-to-end; CI green
1 — Catalog + Bound IR quasar-cat + quasar-ir + quasar-bind + subset quasar-sql; full logical RelExpr/ScalarExpr; name res + type check 3–4 wk scope creep in SQL grammar; correlation/outer-col bugs binder fixtures roundtrip; quasar opt --bind-only dumps typed tree; type errors are OptError::Unsupported w/ location
2 — Logical Props + FDs quasar-props: out_cols, not_null, keys, FuncDepSet + closure, cardinality bounds, outer_cols; OrderingChoice simplification 2–3 wk FD-closure correctness; not-null propagation through outer joins FD/key goldens match; OrderingChoice ⊆ FD closure (I6); cardinality bounds never violated by later stats
3 — The Memo quasar-memo: Group/MExpr/Scalar arenas, interning dedup, group props, normalize-on-construct hook, memo dump 2–3 wk intern-key collisions; arena index misuse across memos shared subtrees collapse to one group (I1); memo-dump goldens; no duplicate members (I2)
4 — optgen + Normalize rules quasar-optgen build-time codegen + quasar-rules[Normalize] (~60 rules): pushdown, folding, pruning, simplification 3–4 wk codegen non-determinism; non-confluent rules → divergence quasar opt --norm + trace; normalize idempotent + confluent (I3); pushdown/pruning goldens; byte-identical codegen
5 — Stats + Cardinality quasar-stats: row counts, HLL NDV, equi-depth histograms, multi-col stats; selectivity + join/group-by cardinality 3–4 wk estimator blind spots; correlated-predicate under/over-estimation q-error within target on TPC-H-lite; selectivity unit goldens; missing-stats path never panics
6 — Constraint/Span Solver quasar-constraint: predicate → spans + remaining; AND/OR/IN/tuple; tight vs loose; partial/inverted 2–3 wk span explosion on big OR/IN; soundness (excluding a valid row) index-constraints goldens (a=5 AND b>10 → [/5/10 - /5]); unsat → empty spans; tightness flagged
7 — Cost Model quasar-cost: per-operator cpu/io/seek/net costs + Coster trait + B&B lower bound; costs.toml calibration 2–3 wk mis-calibrated coefficients; cost non-monotonicity per-node cost in EXPLAIN(OPT); cost monotonicity (I4); merge-join-with-order beats hash on sorted-input fixtures
8 — Exploration Rules quasar-rules[Explore] (~40): join commutativity/associativity + reorder enumeration, GenerateIndexScan, limit pushdown, agg interchange 3–4 wk join-order blowup; equivalence violations explore goldens (orders enumerated, index scans generated); equivalence preserved; reorder-limit fallback logged
9 — Phys Props + Enforcers + Implement quasar-rules[Implement] (~50) + physical-prop framework; Join→Hash/Merge/Lookup, GroupBy→Hash/Stream; Sort/Distribute enforcers; interesting orderings 3–4 wk enforcer-completeness gaps → NoPlanFound; ordering-derivation bugs enforcer fixtures (ORDER BY → Sort; merge join requests sorted children); ordering goldens; required props satisfied (I5)
10 — Cascades Search quasar-search: task stack, branch-and-bound, optimization goals, winner memoization, plan extraction; EXPLAIN(OPT) 4 wk non-termination; B&B suboptimal pruning; nondeterminism lowest-cost plan on fixtures; B&B == exhaustive within budget (I7); deterministic plan (I10); terminates under budget
11 — Distributed Cost + Multi-Region locality-aware net cost, Distribution property + Distribute enforcer cost, LOS, lookup-vs-hash distributed crossover 3–4 wk wrong distribution derivation; LOS correctness; net-cost mis-weighting locality fixtures (placement → different plan); LOS fixture; lookup-vs-hash crossover goldens; root distribution = required (I12)
12 — DistSQL Planning quasar-distsql: physical plan → FlowSpec (processors/streams/routers), leaseholder placement, local-vs-distributed, ordered merges; EXPLAIN(DISTSQL) 3–4 wk router mis-selection; ordering lost across fan-in dist goldens (placement + router types); ordered fan-in preserves order; local-vs-distributed threshold fixture
13 — Cache + Placeholders + Obs + Hardening quasar-cache (prepared stmts, generic/custom, staleness) + quasar-obs (event log, replay) + full conformance + fuzzing + soak 4 wk cache staleness misses; nondeterminism leaks; fuzz-found panics placeholder goldens; staleness invalidation fixture; conformance green; 1-h fuzz zero panics; replay byte-identical

Total: Phase 0 ships in 1–2 weeks; the full Phase 0–13 build is a multi-month effort consistent with §1.

Note: per workspace convention this document is specification only. The — <Language> ✅ tag is earned later, when code is built from this blueprint, make test passes, and the core optimize() path runs end-to-end. Nothing here is compiled or executed; this is the locked blueprint an implementation team builds from.
